## Rules of Conduct for Public Comment at Council Meetings*

Members of the public may speak at a Council meeting upon recognition by the Mayor. Public comment may only occur during periods designated on the agenda for public comment or a public hearing. A person may speak for up to two (2) minutes during each of the two public comment periods on agenda items or non-agenda items. Walvers of the time requirement may only be granted in the discretion of the Mayor, and waivers to speak at a time other than a designated comment period may only be granted by the Council. Any person wishing to make a presentation longer than two minutes or requiring audio-visual equipment is asked to contact the City Clerk requesting to appear on a future agenda.

A person may only address Council from the podium. Only one person may occupy the podium at a time. All remarks are to be directed to the Mayor and Council. Speakers are not to engage in direct dialog with other meeting attendees.

Any person who violates the Rules of Conduct, disturbs the peace at the meeting, and/or interferes with the meeting may be warned, ordered to be seated, removed, and/or ticketed.

 $ ^{*} $This summarizes Council Resolution 04-18. Complete Rules, including guidelines for considering waivers, are available in the Council Chambers and from the City Clerk.

## GASB 75 (OPEB) PRESENTATION

Finance and Benefit Administrator Tiernan gave a brief presentation regarding the GASB 75 Interim Actuarial Evaluation for last fiscal year. They are basically taking the data she provided to them in June 2022 and updating with new interest rates, they supply us with new data for the following fiscal year. Any new hires, and new retirees won't be included in this evaluation, it will be included with the data for the end of this fiscal year. She then explained this presents us looking at our liabilities on the net OPEB predictions. She stated there were no significant assumptions in the current year. They used an inflation assumption of 3%. She then stated our net OPEB decreased by $53,649 and is currently at $397,276 total

## NEW BUSINESS

1. Replacement of loadbreak and meter bay at wastewater treatment plant

Water and Wastewater Superintendent Beason explained they are requesting the purchase and installation of a new primary (13,200) loadbreak (primary switch) and meter bay. The current one malfunctioned during a recent preventative maintenance inspection and has been flagged by DTE. Utilities Instrumentation Service (UIS) has inspected the loadbreak and determined it cannot be repaired due to its age and condition. This equipment is critical and is required to isolate the plant from DTE in emergencies. He stated he has received 2 bids and is recommending the purchase and installation of a new primary loadbreak from DoubleJack Electric Company in the amount of $36,500.00 Councilmember Hansen asked what is its lifetime. Mr. Beason stated this is the original one so approximate 40 years, and the new one will only have 1 year warranty but they are very reliable. Councilmember Kurtzweil asked about the double load. Mr. Beason stated this is a disconnect to de-energize the water plant instead of having to contact DTE. He then stated this is for the portion DTE takes care of. They have to have a way so they can read our usage. He further stated we will be working with DTE on this. We'll have 3 crews, an overhead crew to disconnect overhead wire underground crew, and the meter crew. He stated he is expecting the end of March to early April. Councilmember Bogert asked the nature of the mal-function. Mr. Beason stated it would not close properly, this switch needs to close instantly, and it wouldn't so they walked away from it. He further stated we were doing preventative maintenance when it happened. Councilmember Bogert asked if it was hard wired to mitigate it not closing. Mr. Beason stated it is mechanical, they aren't allowed to touch it because its 13,000 volts. It is supposed to disengage immediately. He further stated the switchgear is on, but we can't disconnect, we would have to call DTE. Councilmember Kivell asked if he is confident DTE will be able to meet our schedules. Mr. Beason stated the electrician we are using has worked with DTE on something similar, they come highly recommended from HRC and they know the schedule so he is confident these are the people we want to work with. Councilmember Bogert asked how the system will be energized during the changeout. Mr. Beason stated it will be on a generator, and there may be another cost because he would like to purchase another generator to be a standby generator. He further stated he will have one on site, and it will be a long day, but they think they can get it done in one day.

2. Purchase and installation of new 750 KVA transformer (13,200/480)

Water and Wastewater Superintendent Beason stated they need to replace a 750DW primary transformer. The current transformer oil reservoir tank is leaking and it is critical to cooling the transformer and ultimately will lead to failure. The current transformer is a reconditioned unit with an original production date of 1971. Considering the age and condition of this a replacement is necessary. We received two bids and recommend that we purchase from DoubleJack Electric for $51,000.00. CM 12-7-23 MOTION TO APPROVE BURGAGE AND RECEIPT

3. Purchase of new 750kw standby commercial generator

Water and Wastewater Superintendent Beason stated they are requesting the purchase of a new 750kw diesel generator located at the wastewater treatment plant. It will replace the current 1980 620kw generator. The current generator is critical to the operations of the wastewater treatment plant during power outages and has been experiencing some mechanical issues in recent years. HRC along with department staff have determined that replacing the current generator is essential to maintain a reliable standby power source. This is a scheduled purchase for this year's budget as a capital improvement. We received 3 bids and are recommending the purchase of the generator from Cummins in the amount of $245,050. Councilmember Bogert stated in the proposal he didn't see anything about removing the existing generator. Mr. Beason stated they will keep the existing generator and the new one will be in a trailer outside. He stated we will use both of them by alternating them. We could get another 6 months or a year, but he knows there are mechanical issues and there are things we cannot buy anymore for it. We are having more and more issues with it. Councilmember Bogert asked if the purchase is turnkey. Mr. Beason stated it is not, there will be more purchases for design, running the lines and things like that. Councilmember Kivell asked if he had any idea what the scrap value would be on the old generator. Mr. Beason stated it might be worth something, but taking out of the building would be an issue. We would have to either remove a wall, or remove the roof to remove it. Councilmember Kivell stated he is worried about how much money we will spend to try to keep it running. Mr. Beason stated we will not maintain it anymore. It would move to the newer one. Councilmember Kivell stated he doesn't know why we would give up the space for something that isn't working anymore. Mr. Beason stated it is a tight space, we couldn't put the new generator in that space, it isn't feasible, so it will be outside.

4. Resolution approving city hall as early voting location for 2024 elections

Clerk Deaton stated with the approval of proposal 22-02 early voting is now required for all State and Federal Elections. We are asking to use South Lyon City Hall, 335 S Warren, as our 9 days early voting site for 8 hours each day. Councilmember Kurtzweil thanked Clerk Deaton for keeping the early voting here in the city of South Lyon. She is active on the Elections and she has spoken with individuals that had early voting in their city, and some were merged areas and they were not happy. She can understand why. We traditionally vote in the community we live in, and people were hesitant when people were told to go to another community to vote. She further stated there were some procedural issues as well. She further stated our residents deserve to be able to vote in our community and not have to go elsewhere. She then stated nationally early voting wasn't that overwhelming to Clerk's which was surprising, and even though early voting is available, it was not used, absentee ballots were used. It won't be overwhelming to city hall.

Fiscal year 2022 State and Local Cybersecurity grant program (SLCGP) local consent

## agreement

Finance and Benefit Administrator Tiernan stated the SLCGP is administered by the Department of Homeland Security and funded by the Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act. The State of Michigan was awarded $4.7 million for the federal fiscal year 2022. The SLCGP is a reimbursable pass-through grant program with an overall goal to improve the cybersecurity of state, local and territorial governments by providing assistance for managing and reducing systemic cyber risk through the following programs: Endpoint Detection and Response Software licenses, Cybersecurity assessments, and incident response planning and training activities. She further stated they haven't appropriated the funds yet, and there should be no cost to the city. If anything, a 10% match and it may be reimbursable. Our chances are good, and this is the next step in the process to see if we receive it. Councilmember Bogert stated as a software developer by trade, he cannot stress enough how important this is. There has been a lot of incidents with health care systems and even casinos that have been brought down by external threats. CM 12-10-23 MOTION TO APPROVE SLCGP LOCAL CONSENT AGREEMENT

Motion to approve the proposed fiscal year 2022 State and Local Cybersecurity Grant Program Local Consent Agreement

## PUBLIC COMMENT

Sharon of 9729 Ponderosa stated on behalf of all the nonprofits, she thanked everyone from the community. This is an amazing town, and she met with the City Manager and talked about connecting the city with Active Faith, then she met with Nate, and then they were part of concerts in the park, and ladies' night. It has meant a lot to our staff, and what we do feel heavy at times, but there is so many things we tie into like Pumpkinfest, Pumpkinfest Live, Cool Yule, the Carl and Joanne Foundation, the First United Methodist Church, and the lighting of the tree. ## MANAGER'S REPORT

City Manager Zelenak stated on a future agenda we will have the agreement between the schools and the operations of the Senior Center and the city personnel policy. He then stated the Hagadorn and Sanitary Sewer project is moving forward with the clean up and laying top soil, gravel and landscape material on site. He then updated Council on the CSX sanitary sewer line that during inspection and installation, they discovered additional wear and tear on a sewer pipe along Pontiac Trail. The engineer is recommending that we line this additional section of pipe as part of this project, which will add about $35,000 to the project. He then stated the West Lake Development will be on an agenda in January. He stated he has met with the person interested in the 318 W Lake Street property and he has inspected the sanitary sewer line, and he has met with them to discuss their plans for the site, and a copy of the plans, agreement and resolution will be brought to Council on January. He then reminded Council to give him any budget suggestions they may have by January 8th. He then stated they have begun work on Lafayette and Volunteer Park and the parking lot at Volunteer Park has been installed, and they are now grading the new fields. He then reminded everyone the city offices will be closed on December 25, 26, and January 1 and 2nd. Councilmember Kurtzwell stated she has a dilemma which is we had a water rate increase of roughly 8% and now we have the school district going after a mill for non-homestead property and she believes Colonial Acres is non-homestead, which means they would be subject to the increase in the school district as well as the district is asking for. That also includes apartments and if there is going to be an increase in the budget, she will have a hard time approving an increase, especially if the school's mill gets past. Mayor Kennedy asked that we are relining the existing line as opposed to one that was said to have already been done. City Manager Zelenak stated they have not started it yet, the line they are lining wasn't videoed because it would have required a major bypass. This line runs between the new manhole to the other manhole that went under the railroad tracks, it is a few hundred feet section. Councilmember Kennedy stated this may be the same location we did in the past. He also wants a timeline for relining the existing one in addition to the new one. City Manager Zelenak stated we plan on that within the next week and completion within 2 weeks. Mr. Alix of HRC stated he is a spoke at the last meeting, as of the last meeting both lines were up and running, but we are now ready to reline the pipe. Mr. Darga of HRC stated we put a new manhole over the existing line and that line was the one that was taken out previously when they had all the

<a id="p10"></a>
*[Page 10 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=10)* (read by OCR; check the original)

dewatering equipment there. Everyone was under the impression that line was in place all the way to the railroad tracks. They thought the line was PVC, but when they put the manhole in the contractor noticed it was a concrete pipe and it was in bad condition. So, they looked at it and said we should line it or there will be trouble down the road. The current project was from the tracks, under the tracks and along side the tracks. He further stated since they only did spot repair about 40 feet and not and not the 120 feet, we are now going to line everything while they are there. It makes sense because they have all the equipment and it's going to include a bypass and if we don't do it now, we'll have to pay more later.

Councilmember Kennedy asked how do they redirect the flow between one line and the other. He further stated they will plug the one line and it will automatically go to the other line. Councilmember Kivell asked if we were going to lean on the contractor to make sure they remove all the iron and the stuff on Chester and that corridor so it will be done before it snows. City Manager Zelenak stated they are working on that now. Mr. Alix stated they will be back in the spring to make sure everything is completed such as making sure the grass seed took. Councilmember Kivell stated he is more concerned about the iron around the intersection and that should be a priority.

## COUNCIL COMMENTS

Councilmember Hansen stated he and his family enjoyed checking out the Christmas lights in the neighborhoods and he encourages folks to enjoy the holiday spirit. He then wished everyone a Happy New Year, Merry Christmas and a happy holiday season.

Councilmember Dilg stated the events downtown looked amazing last weekend. She then thanked Dayna Johnston, and Heinanen. She then thanked Nate and everyone that made an effort to make it happen. It was amazing all the work that went into that. She then thanked Tracey Smith for her work on the DDA. She then congratulated First United Methodist Church on their 190 years. She also congratulated the Pumpkinfest Committee and its amazing they were able to give $12,000 to local non profits and it was fun to hear about surprising some of the groups. She then congratulated Julie Paquette and the Friends of Roper Barn for the successful fundraiser and it's great to find fun ways to fund the barn. She then stated South Lyon is an amazing place to live and play.

Councilmember Kurtzweil congratulated First United Methodist Church where she has many friends. She stated they are a great community and she has enjoyed the friendships there for many years. The proclamation couldn't go to a better organization at this time for all they give to the community and it is very commendable, and particularly to the Kutesel's, the amount of time they have donated to the community over the decades on behalf of First United Methodist Church is incredible and she thanked them for everything they do. She then sent a message of support and love to the women that sent her a letter after her vote at the last meeting for Haven. She stated it was a touching letter and she appreciates it very much. She then stated journeys are journeys and you move forward with your life and there are many individuals out there that will be with you. She further stated they didn't sign their names to the letter, but she will advocate and she understands you move quietly in the community to bring attention to domestic and sexual violence, and hang in there, life has you for a certain reason and you are strong and you'll survive. Merry Christmas to the ladies that sent her the letter. She then stated Happy 5th day of Hannukah to our friends, neighbors and business owners. She then stated she hopes everyone gets out and enjoys the holidays in South Lyon and peace and comfort to everyone.

Councilmember Bogert stated he had the opportunity to participate in last weekend's holiday events and it was amazing to see the turnout and he went to the Christmas market and there were a lot of vendors and a lot of people shopping and both tree lightings went on without a hitch and he wants to acknowledge

<a id="p11"></a>
*[Page 11 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=11)* (read by OCR; check the original)

everyone that was involved in the holiday celebrations. He then stated he hopes everyone will shop local for the holidays.

Councilmember Kivell stated he read a post for Kiwanis and they said they have trees available and if someone didn't have the money to get one, they are welcome to get one and enjoy their Christmas.

Mayor Kennedy reminded everyone about the South Lyon Eve event on December 31st at Heinanen Engineering. It starts at 7pm with a beer tent and entertainment from Power Play Detroit. So, come on out and watch the ball drop on New Year's Eve. He then wished everyone a very Merry Christmas, a Happy Hanukkah, a Joyous Kwanza or, in the words of Jerry Stiller from the Seinfeld Show and I quote, "A Happy Festivus for the rest of us" He then wished everyone a great holiday and a happy new year.

ADJOURNMENT

CM 12-11-23 MOTION TO ADJOURN

Motion by Kurtzweil, supported by Dilg

Motion to adjourn at 8:49 p.m.

VOTE: ___

MOTION CARRIED UNANIMOUSLY

Respectfully submitted,

Mayor Steve Kennedy

Clerk/Treasurer Lisa Deaton

## SOUTH LYON AREA PUMPKINFEST GIVES BACK

# AGENDA NOTE Consent Agenda Item #1

MEETING DATE: January 22, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Clerk

AGENDA TOPIC: 2024 City Council meeting schedule

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: MCL 15.265(2) provides:

(2) For regular meetings of a public body, there shall be posted within 10 days after the first meeting of the public body in each calendar or fiscal year a public notice stating the dates, times, and places of its regular meetings.

The City posts a notice outside on the city hall bulletin board that regular council meetings are held on the 2nd and 4th Mondays of each month.

A quick review of the calendar shows the following as Regular Council Meetings

JANUARY 8 & 22

FEBRUARY 12 & 26

MARCH 11 & 25

APRIL 8 & 22

MAY 13 & 28

JUNE 10 & 24

JULY 8 & 22

AUGUST 12 & 26

SEPTEMBER 9 & 23

OCTOBER 14 & 28

NOVEMBER 11 & 25

DECEMBER 9 & 23

A simple motion to approve the dates for regular council meetings on the 2nd and 4th Mondays of each month.

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: N/A

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Approve the above listed Council meeting dates for 2024

SUGGESTED MOTION: Moved by, _____ seconded by, _____ to approve the dates for regular council meetings on the 2nd and 4th Mondays of each month.

# AGENDA NOTE Unfinished Business #1

MEETING DATE: January 22, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: City Manager

AGENDA TOPIC: Road Improvement Plan Discussion 2024 Projects

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: Tonight we will briefly discuss the planned road improvements for 2024. Brookfield and Eagle Pointe areas will be the next projects in our multi-year Road Improvement Program. This project will entail repairs of certain sections of concrete roads and reconstruction of Brookfield Drive, and reconstruction of all roads within Eagle Pointe subdivision. Preliminary cost estimate for both projects will be approximately $3.5 – 4.0 million dollars. We have sent out letters to both subdivisions informing them of the meeting date to discuss the planned improvements. (February 1, 2024) At that meeting we will discuss bid dates, and construction dates as well. With a follow-up meeting in April with construction plans and more detailed information. Currently we are gathering topo information on both subs.

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: Road improvement program map and information.

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Discussion / questions on the City's Road Improvement Plan for 2024.

# AGENDA NOTE New Business: Item #1

MEETING DATE: January 22, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Jeff Archey, DPW Superintendent

AGENDA TOPIC: Purchase of an F-350 pickup truck with a snow plow.

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: The DPW is requesting the purchase an F-350 with a snow plow from Varsity Ford. This vehicle will be replacing our 16 year old F-350 truck with a snow plow. The vehicle that we are replacing will be placed on an auction site. The value of the old truck would be $1000 or less. This is budgeted item at $50,000 for the current FY and would be ready for delivery in January of 2024. A budget amendment will be required for the increased price difference of $8,444. The funds for this purchase will come out of the Vehicle/Equipment Replacement fund 642-979.300.

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: Quotation from Varsity Ford and 2 from Brighton Ford. Also included is the invoice to put the plow on the F-350.

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Approve purchase/do not approve purchase of the F-350 with a plow from Varsity Ford for $58,444.00 from GL account 642-979.300

SUGGESTED MOTION: Motion by ___, supported by

 $ \underline{\hspace{2em}} $ to approve the purchase of the F-350 with a plow from Varsity Ford for 58,444.00 from GL account 642-979.300.

Motion by ___, supported by ___, to amend the FY 2023-2024 Budget under account line item 642-979.300 from $50,000 to $58,444.

# AGENDA NOTE New Business Item #2

MEETING DATE: January 22, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Recommendation from Planning Commission on November 16, 2023

AGENDA TOPIC: First Reading of Ordinance to Amend the Official Zoning Map of the City of South Lyon by conditionally rezoning portions of the total 34.17+/- acre parcel on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Ten Mile/Lake Street, parcel # 21-30-126-003, zoned I-1, Light Industrial, as follows: 24.39+/- acres of RM-3, Multiple-Family Residential and 1.63+/- acres R-3, Single-Family Residential. The remaining 8.14+/- acres will remain as I-1, Light Industrial.

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: Elevate Land Holdings, LLC has petitioned the City and requested a Conditional Rezoning of a portion of a 34.17+/- acre parcel on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Ten Mile/Lake Street, parcel #21-30-126-003, to allow 252 new multiple-family residential units, 3 single-family residential lots, and the remaining portion of the parcel to be developed with approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial development. The petitioner's request is pursuant to MCL 125.3405, which authorizes rezoning with conditions voluntarily offered by the owner of the property. The Planning Commission held a public hearing for the request on November 16, 2023 and recommended approval of the request for the conditional rezoning based on the conditions voluntarily offered in writing by the owner.

A conditional rezoning agreement has been prepared and the City Attorney and applicant attorney have worked together to produce a draft agreement for City Council consideration.  $ \underline{\text{The application also includes a Preliminary Site Plan for approval, which is incorporated}} $ as an exhibit of the conditional rezoning agreement.

The conditions voluntarily offered by the applicant are listed under Paragraph 2 of the conditional rezoning agreement.

If the conditional rezoning request is approved, the development and use of the property must be in conformance with the preliminary site plan approved as an exhibit of the agreement, and must be in compliance with the conditions of the rezoning as written in the conditional rezoning agreement.

The process of the conditional rezoning is a four-step process:

1. Public hearing and review of the conditional rezoning request and preliminary site plan by the Planning Commission with a recommendation to City Council;

2. Review and action by the City Council on the conditional rezoning request and preliminary site plan. If City Council approves, first reading of the Zoning Ordinance amendment;

<a id="p58"></a>
*[Page 58 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=58)* (read by OCR; check the original)

3. Second reading of the Zoning Ordinance amendment and final approval of the Conditional Rezoning Agreement by City Council;

4. Review and action on final site plan by the Planning Commission.

Step #1 was completed on November 16, 2023 and the Planning Commission recommended approval of the conditional rezoning and approval of the preliminary site plan. This is currently at Step #2 for City Council action on the conditional rezoning request and preliminary site plan. If approved, it will be the first reading of the ordinance amendment for the conditional rezoning. The ordinance amendment and conditional rezoning agreement would then come back to Council for second reading and approval of the agreement. Following that, the final site plan would be submitted in line with the agreement and be reviewed by the Planning Commission.

## MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS:

- Ordinance to amend the official zoning map of the City of South Lyon conditionally rezoning portions of parcel # 21-30-126-003 from I-1 Light Industrial, including 24.39+/- acres of RM-3, Multiple-Family Residential and 1.63+/- acres R-3, Single-Family Residential. The proposed ordinance contains the proposed Conditional Rezoning Agreement verbatim, but the Agreement will be presented separately for approval.

CIB Planning Review dated 11/15/2023

- HRC Review dated 7/25/2023

- Fire Department Review dated 8/15/2023

• Building Department Review dated 7/20/2023

• Updated Conditional Rezoning Application

• Updated Conditional Rezoning Narrative dated 11/9/2023

- Preliminary Site Plans dated 11/8/2023 – full site plan set in 24x36 hard copy format

- Copy of applicant presentation slides

• Residential Market Analysis provided by applicant dated 8/7/23

Traffic Impact Study provided by applicant dated 9/21/23

Updated wetland crossing cross sections dated 1/4/24

Supplemental traffic memo on entrance location dated 1/2/24

• Draft Planning Commission minutes dated 11/16/2023

Public comments submitted for Council consideration

WHEREAS, the Property is currently zoned I-1 (Light Industrial) and Owner desires to develop a mixed-use development to include a Multiple Family Residential and a Single Family Residential development project on a portion of the Property which is not permitted under the Property's I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning district, and to develop a portion of the Property in conformance with the Property's existing I-1 Light Industrial zoning classification;

WHEREAS, pursuant to Section 405 of the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act, 2008 PA 110, MCL 125.3405 et seq., as amended, certain conditions voluntarily offered by the Owner of the Property, may become a condition of rezoning the Property;

WHEREAS, on or about June 6, 2023, a representative of the Owner, submitted a Conditional Rezoning Application for the Property along with a preliminary site plan, a written supplement, preliminary architectural drawings, and a preliminary landscape plan, voluntarily offering, in writing, certain conditions to rezone the Property to PUD (Planned Unit Development);

WHEREAS, on September 28, 2023, the City Planning Commission held a public hearing, after publication of notice thereof, on the Owner's Conditional Rezoning Application;

WHEREAS, on September 28, 2023, the City Planning Commission recommended approval of the Owner's request for conditional rezoning based upon the conditions voluntarily offered by Owner in writing;

WHEREAS, after receiving a favorable recommendation from the Planning Commission, but before the recommendation was presented to City Council, the Owner, City Planner, City Attorney and City Manager reevaluated the Owner's request to conditionally rezone to PUD (Planned Unit Development) and agreed that the conditional rezoning should have been to RM-3 (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 (Single Family Residential) for a portion of the Property.

*[Page 65 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=65)* (read by OCR; check the original)

WHEREAS, on _____ 2024, the City Council approved the request for Conditional Rezoning and adopted Ordinance No. _____ based upon the conditions voluntarily offered by the Owner in writing which are set forth in this Agreement and the attached Exhibit A; and

NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the foregoing and the mutual promises hereinafter set forth, the City and Owner agree as follows:

1.  $ \underline{\text{Conditional Rezoning}} $. Pursuant to Section 405 of the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act, MCL 125.3405, Owner agrees to limit the development and use of the Property as set forth in the rezoning conditions below as a condition of rezoning. Upon execution of this Agreement by the parties, the City shall effectuate the conditional rezoning of the Residential Property from I-1 District (Limited Industrial) to the RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 District (Single Family Residential), as legally described herein, on the City's official Zoning Map. This Agreement is valid and entered into on a voluntary basis and represents a permissible exercise of authority by the City pursuant to MCL 125.3405.

2.  $ \underline{\text{Rezoning Conditions}} $. Owner agrees to develop and use the Property in accordance with the following conditions which have been offered voluntarily in writing by the Owner as conditions to the rezoning:

a. Development and use of the Property shall be for a phased development of Multiple Family Residential and Single Family Residential on the Residential Property, and a phased development of Industrial buildings on the remaining portion of the Property zoned I-1 District (Light Industrial) in substantial conformance with the preliminary site plan, attached as Exhibit B, and in accordance with the Time Limits described in Paragraph 6, below, consisting of:

(1) 24.40\pm/ - acres of Multiple Family Residential Property, 1.63\pm/ - acres of Single-Family Residential Property and 8.14\pm/ - acres of Light Industrial Property;

(2) 252 Multiple Family 1-, 2-, and 3-bedroom residential units on the Property rezoned RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential);

(3) 3 single family residential detached dwelling units on the Property rezoned R-3 District (Single Family Residential); and

<a id="p66"></a>
*[Page 66 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=66)* (read by OCR; check the original)

(4) Approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial building space on the I-1 District (Light Industrial).

(collectively referred to as the "Project"). The Project will be developed in the phases as depicted on the preliminary site plan attached as Exhibit B and as follows: Phase 1 of the Industrial development, shall include one 19,840 +/- sq. ft. industrial building; Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 4-7, 84 units total; Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 8-12, 84 units total, and the clubhouse with ancillary amenities; Phase 3 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 13-18, 84 units total. The future development of the remaining I-1 (Light Industrial) zoned property and the R-3 (Single Family Residential) zoned property will be developed in accordance with the underlying zoning district.

b. Development of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential development to occur concurrently.

c. Constructed as part of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential development, the paving of 1,088 lineal feet of Dixboro Road from Lake Street to the southerly end of the Property.

d. At the time of Dixboro Road paving, the pedestrian pathway ultimately connecting to Huron Valley Trail, measuring 1,088 lineal feet along Dixboro Rd, will be installed from Lake Street to the southerly end of the Property line;

e. As part of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential Development, a payment of $36,200 to the City of South Lyon will be made for the future construction of the remaining 724 lineal feet from the southerly Property line and Huron Valley Trail.

f. As a part of Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential development, a pedestrian pathway (connecting to the pedestrian pathway constructed in Phase 1) will be constructed along Lake Street from the corner of Lake Street and Dixboro Road to South Hagadorn Road connecting to the existing sidewalk that leads to downtown.

g. Provided that all necessary permits are secured by the applicable state, county, city or other governmental agency, the existing Lake Street driveway approach serving Westlake Place Apartments (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005) will be removed and relocated during Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential development in order to improve public and traffic safety. If an amendment to the final approved site plan is required, it shall be considered a minor revision.

h. Owner shall secure and record all necessary shared access, use, utility, facilities, parking and other easements between the Project and adjacent development Westlake Place Apartments (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005) as required by and in a form approved by the City.

i. Minimum floor area reduction to allow for 1-bedroom units to be a minimum of 600 sq. ft., 2-bedroom units to be a minimum of 768 sq. ft. and 3-bedroom units to be a minimum of 600 sq. ft.

<a id="p67"></a>
*[Page 67 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=67)* (read by OCR; check the original)

a minimum of 936 sq. ft. to meet market demand for 1 - 3 bedroom units and to allow preservation of nine (9) acres of wetlands and 50% of the existing woodlands.

j. 572 parking spaces (which includes parking spaces within the existing Westlake Place Apartments, (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005), which will be available for use within the Project) provided for use by the Multiple Family Residential Property as recommended in the traffic study conducted by Fleis and Vandenbrink, which reduction will reduce impervious surfaces, increase available open space and area for community garden, recreation courts and a dog park.

k. 223 parking spaces provided for the industrial use to be located on the portion of the Property within the I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning district. 67 parking spaces shall be provided for the Phase 1 Industrial development and the additional parking spaces are proposed because tenants and/or users have not been identified or secured for the Industrial use. Once a site plan is submitted for the specific configuration of the remaining Light Industrial district, the City shall determine the required number of parking spaces required or shall cause the Owner to conduct a parking and/or traffic study to establish a basis for the City to consider the appropriate number of parking spaces required. If an amendment to the final approved site plan is required, it shall be considered a minor revision.

For the efficient use of the Property, considering the Planned Unit Development meets several master plan goals, the buffering requirement of the City's Zoning Ordinance between industrial and residential uses must be waived and, once tenants are secured and a site plan submitted, the City shall determine the appropriate buffer requirement considering the proposed industrial use.

3.  $ \underline{\text{Use of the Property}} $. Development and use of the Property shall be in substantial conformance with the approved preliminary site plan, attached as Exhibit B, and shall be subject to and in conformance with the approval of the final site plan for the Property by the City Planning Commission. The parties agree that minor revisions to the approved final site plan which, in the sole discretion of the City Manager, do not significantly modify the proposed development or use of the Property or the site plan, may be considered and approved by the City administratively without the necessity of review and approval by the Planning Commission and/or without amendment of this Agreement and exhibits hereto.

4.  $ \underline{\text{Compliance with Laws}} $. Except as modified herein, development and use of the Property shall comply with all other applicable state, county and local requirements for land

<a id="p68"></a>
*[Page 68 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=68)* (read by OCR; check the original)

development and use, ordinances, codes, standards, regulations, requirements, and engineering design standards and requirements.

5.  $ \underline{\text{Compliance with Conditions}} $. Once commenced and established, the development and use of the Property must remain in compliance with the conditions of the conditional rezoning Final Site Plan and as provided in this Agreement. Any failure to comply with a condition contained herein shall constitute a violation of the City of South Lyon Zoning Ordinance and shall be punishable as provided for therein. Additionally, any such violation shall be deemed a nuisance per se and subject to abatement as provided for by law.

6.  $ \underline{\text{Time Limits}} $. The City and Owner agree that the following approvals and activities are conditions to this Agreement and if not completed within the following time limits, the Residential Property may revert back to the I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning classification:

a. Final site plan approval within twelve (12) months from the date of this Agreement;

b. A building permit for the Phase 1 Multiple Family Residential and the Phase 1 Industrial development thereof, if applicable, is obtained within one (1) year from the date of final site plan approval;

c. A building permit for Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential is obtained within twenty-four (24) months of the issuance of the first building permit for Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential.

d. A building permit for Phase 3 of the Multiple-Family Residential is obtained within twenty-four (24) months of the issuance of the first building permit for Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential.

e. A final certificate of occupancy for the entire Multiple Family Residential, or Phase 2, or Phase 3 thereof if applicable, is obtained within thirty (30) months from the date the building permit was issued for said Phase.

7.  $ \underline{\text{Extension of Time Limits}} $. The City, in the City's exclusive discretion, may agree to extend the time limits in Paragraph 6 upon receipt of a written request from the Owner but the City shall not be required to grant such request for extension. Any extension granted shall be in writing signed by both parties.

<a id="p69"></a>
*[Page 69 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=69)* (read by OCR; check the original)

8.  $ \underline{\text{City Right to Rezone}} $. In the event that the time limit conditions in Paragraph 6 are not met, nor extended upon mutual agreement of the parties pursuant to Paragraph 7; nothing in this Agreement shall be deemed to prohibit the City from rezoning all or any portion of the Property which is subject to this Agreement to another zoning district. Any rezoning shall be conducted in compliance with the City's Zoning Ordinance and the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act.

9. Owner Acknowledgments. Owner acknowledges that it voluntarily offered the conditions contained in this Agreement, including all Exhibits, in connection with the request for conditional rezoning of the Property and that such conditions were offered in writing. Owner acknowledges that upon the approval of the requested conditional rezoning and this Agreement becoming effective, the development and use of the Property shall conform thereafter with the terms and conditions of the conditional rezoning, this Agreement, and all the requirements regulating development and use within the zoning district as modified by the voluntarily offered conditions and with this Agreement and that any failure to fully comply with such conditions and/or this Agreement shall constitute a violation of the City of South Lyon Zoning Ordinance, and shall be punishable accordingly. Owner acknowledges and agrees that the terms and conditions of this Agreement are necessary and roughly proportional to the burden imposed by the conditional rezoning of the Property, and are therefore necessary to ensure that public services and facilities will be capable of accommodating the development and use and the increased service of facility loads caused by the development and use, to protect the natural environment and conserve natural resources, to ensure compatibility with adjacent uses of land, and to promote the use of the Property in a socially and economically feasible manner, and to achieve other legitimate objectives authorized by law. Owner agrees that the terms and conditions herein do not constitute a taking of property for any purpose or a violation of any

<a id="p70"></a>

Constitutional right, and Owner agrees to be bound by each and every provision and conditions of this Agreement. Owner acknowledges and agrees that if the conditional rezoning becomes void for any reason, then no further development shall take place and no permits shall be issued unless and until a new zoning district classification for the Property has been established.

10.  $ \underline{\text{Entire Agreement}} $. This Agreement together with any agreements referenced herein, constitutes the entire agreement between the parties with respect to the subject of this Agreement.

11.  $ \underline{\text{Binding Effect, Running with the Land}} $. This Agreement shall be binding on and inure to the benefit of the parties and their respective successors, heirs, assigns and transferees, and shall run with the land.

12.  $ \underline{\text{Amendments}} $. The terms of this Agreement may be amended, changed, or modified, but only by written agreement executed by the required parties. Any amendment of this Agreement shall be recorded in the Oakland County Register of Deeds.

13.  $ \underline{\text{Governing Law}} $. This Agreement shall be governed by, construed, and enforced in accordance with Michigan law.

14.  $ \underline{\text{Authority to Execute}} $. The parties each represent and state that the individuals

signing this Agreement are fully authorized to execute this document and bind their respective

parties to the terms and conditions contained herein.

15.  $ \underline{\text{Enforcement}} $. In the event of a proceeding to enforce this Agreement, a prevailing party may seek to recover its costs and attorney fees in addition to any other applicable and available relief.

16.  $ \underline{\text{Joint Drafting}} $. No provision of this Agreement shall be construed against or interpreted to the disadvantage of one party against any other party by any court or other

<a id="p71"></a>
*[Page 71 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=71)* (read by OCR; check the original)

governmental authority by reason of any determination or assertion that one party was chiefly or primarily responsible for having drafted this Agreement.

17.  $ \underline{\text{Conflicts}} $. In the event of conflict between the provisions of this Agreement and the provisions of another applicable ordinance, code, regulations, requirement, standard, or policy, the provisions of this Agreement shall prevail.

18.  $ \underline{\text{Severability}} $. This invalidity of any provision of this Agreement shall not affect the validity of the remaining provisions, which shall remain valid and enforceable to the fullest extent permitted by law.

19.  $ \underline{\text{Recording}} $. This Agreement shall be binding on the parties and the Property and shall be recorded with the Oakland County Register of Deeds.

20.  $ \underline{\text{Counterparts}} $. This Agreement and any amendments to it may be executed in multiple counterparts, each of which shall be deemed an original and all of which shall constitute one Agreement. The signature of any party to any counterpart shall be deemed to be a signature to, and may be appended to, any other counterpart.

November 15, 2023

Planning Commission

City of South Lyon

335 South Warren Street

South Lyon, Michigan 48178

## Subject:

REVISED - Westlake Conditional Rezoning Review Light Industrial I-1, to Multiple Family, RM-3 and Single Family Residential, R-3. Approximately 34.42 $ \pm $/- acre parcel located on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Lake Street (Ten Mile), Parcel #21-30-126-003.

Dear Planning Commissioners:

Elevate Land Holdings LLC, a Delaware Limited Liability Company, has petitioned the City and requested a Conditional Rezoning of a portion of a 34.17+/-acre parcel of industrially zoned land on Dixboro Road, parcel # 21-30-126-003, to allow 252 new multiple family residential units, 3 single family residential lots and the remaining portion of the parcel be developed with approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial development. To allow for this, the request is to rezone 24.40+/- acres to RM-3, Multiple-Family Residential, 1.63+/- acres to R-3, Single Family Residential, and the remaining 8.14+/- acres to remain I-1, Light Industrial. The request is pursuant to M.C.L. 125.3405.

The Michigan Zoning Enabling statute allows for the conditional rezoning of land offering another tool for property owners seeking the ability to use their property in a way other than what is allowed by current zoning. If approved, a conditional rezoning ties the use and any development of a property to specific conditions offered by the property owner/applicant.

M.C.L 125.3405 Use and development of land as condition to rezoning.

Sec. 405. (1) An owner of land may voluntarily offer in writing, and the local unit of government may approve, certain use and development of the land as a condition to a rezoning of the land or an amendment to a zoning map.

In approving the conditions under subsection (1), the local unit of government may establish a time period during which the conditions apply to the land. Except for an extension under subsection (4), if the conditions are not satisfied within the time specified under this subsection, the land shall revert to its former zoning classification.

The local government shall not add to or alter the conditions approved under subsection (1) during the time period specified under subsection (2) of this section.

The time period specified under subsection (2) may be extended upon the application of the landowner and approval of the local unit of government.

<a id="p78"></a>
*[Page 78 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=78)* (read by OCR; check the original)

A local unit of government shall not require a landowner to offer conditions as a requirement for rezoning. The lack of an offer under subsection (1) shall not otherwise affect a landowner's rights under this act, the ordinances of the local unit of government, or any other laws of this state.

CONDITIONAL REZONING PROCESS

The process to review and approve a Conditional Rezoning has four (4) stages:

Step 1: Public

Hearing, PC Review

and

Recommendation

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

……

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

 $ \frac{1}{2}x_{n} - \frac{1}{2}x_{n-1} $

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

Step 1: Public Hearing, Conditional Rezoning Review, Preliminary Site Plan/Conceptual Plan Review, and Conditional Rezoning Agreement draft recommendation from the Planning Commission to the City Council

Step 2: Council's first reading of the Conditional Rezoning, Preliminary site plan/conceptual plan approval and Conditional Rezoning Agreement

Step 3: Council's second reading of the Conditional Rezoning and approval of Conditional Rezoning Agreement.

Step 4: Final Site plan review and approval by the Planning Commission.

This review letter will first address the rezoning portion of the request and then the development and site plan review that accompanies the rezoning.

## PROPERTY BACKGROUND

The applicant is requesting to rezone a portion of the 34.17-acre parcel, parcel # 21-301-26-003, l-1, Light Industrial, to RM-3, Multiple Family and R-3, Single Family Residential. The application submittal for the Conditional Rezoning has an accompanying Preliminary Site Plan and Conditional Rezoning Agreement.

<a id="p79"></a>
*[Page 79 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=79)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## Previous Rezoning attempts for parcel #21-301-26-003:

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

This property was rezoned from Residential to Industrial in 1989 at the request of the previous property owner. A site plan for a light industrial park with 11 units, commonly known as West End Industrial, was approved in 1999 and construction on the park started in 2003. The utilities were installed, and the road constructed, however, no further development occurred, and the site sat dormant. In 2016, the property owner requested that the property be rezoned back to residential use, from 1-1, Light Industrial, to RM-1, Multiple Family Residential. The Planning Commission recommended denial of the request, based on the following: the proposed rezoning is not consistent with the Master Plan; there is a need to retain the City's limited industrial land; and the site is capable of accommodating the range of uses permitted in the I-1 District. The City Council unanimously denied the application in 2017. Elevate Properties has now requested a Conditional Rezoning to develop the property with both multiple family residential and industrial.

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

<a id="p80"></a>
*[Page 80 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=80)* (read by OCR; check the original)

CONDITIONAL REZONING REVIEW AND ANALYSIS

The applicant is proposing to change the zoning of a portion of parcel 21-30-126-003 from Light Industrial, I-1, to Multiple Family Residential, RM-3, and Single Family Residential, R-3, as a Conditional Rezoning under Section 405 of the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act (Act 110 of 2006). A Preliminary Site Plan is required to illustrate the proposed development and show that ordinance requirements can be met. Under Section 405 of the Zoning Enabling Act, a petitioner may voluntarily offer in writing, and the City may approve, certain uses and development of property as a condition to rezoning land. The conditions proposed by a petitioner, as part of a conditional rezoning, are supposed to result in recognizable and material benefits to the City that would unlikely be achieved otherwise under the site's existing zoning.

## As part of this conditional rezoning request, the petitioner is voluntarily offering the following:

1. Phased development of a Mixed-Use Development consisting of:

a. 24.40\pm/- acres of multiple family residential, 1.63\pm/- acres of single family residential, and 8.14\pm/- acres of light industrial;

b. 252 new multiple residential units, consisting of 1-, 2-, and 3-bedroom residential units;

c. 3 single family residential detached dwelling units;

d. Approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial building space on remaining industrially zoned property.

2. Development of Phase 1 portion of the Industrial and Phase 1 Multiple Family Residential concurrently;

3. Constructed as part of Phase 1 Industrial and Phase 1 Multiple Family Residential development, paving of 1,088 lineal feet of Dixboro Road from Lake Street to the southerly end of the property.

4. At the time of Dixboro Road paving, the pedestrian pathway ultimately connecting to Huron Valley Trail, measuring 1,088 lineal feet along Dixboro Rd, will be installed from Lake Street to the southerly end of the property line;

5. As part of Phase 1 development, a payment of $36,200 to the City of South Lyon will be made for the future construction of the remaining 724 lineal feet from southerly property line to Huron Valley Trail.

6. As part of Phase 2 Multiple Family Residential development, a pedestrian pathway (connecting to the pedestrian pathway constructed in Phase 1) will be constructed along Lake Street from the corner of Lake Street and Dixboro Road to South Hagadorn Road connecting to the existing sidewalk that leads to downtown.

7. Provided that all necessary permits are secured by the applicable state, county, city or other governmental agency, the existing Lake Street driveway approach serving Westlake Place Apartments (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005) will be removed and relocated during Phase 2 in order to improve public and traffic safety. If an amendment to the final approved site plan is required, it shall be considered a minor revision.

<a id="p81"></a>
*[Page 81 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=81)* (read by OCR; check the original)

LOCATION AND DESCRIPTION

The site is surrounded by varied uses – to the north is single-family residence and a multiple-family residential apartment complex, West Lake Apartment, owned by the applicant; to the west are single-family residential homes (in Green Oak Township); industrial to the south (Michigan Seamless Tube and the City's Wastewater Treatment Facility) and east (Michigan Seamless Tube). The property has frontage on both Dixboro Road and Lake Street (Ten Mile Road). Dixboro Road divides the City and Green Oak Township. Dixboro Road is not paved, and while many discussions have indicated that it will be paved, the timing of that construction is unknown. A portion of this parcel was cleared and prepared for industrial development, including installation of utilities and a road. A significant portion of the parcel contains wetlands with topography falling to the north and east.

<a id="p85"></a>
*[Page 85 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=85)* (read by OCR; check the original)

As this is a rezoning request, consideration should be given to the proposed use(s) as it relates to the surrounding zoning and land uses, existing infrastructure, and most importantly consistency with the Master Plan.

CONDITIONAL REZONING REVIEW STANDARDS

In reviewing this application, the Planning Commission should consider additional comments from the applicant, relevant factual new information presented at the public hearing, and the following criteria, among other factors they may deem appropriate, in making their findings, decision and corresponding recommendation, based upon Section 102-213-Criteria for Amendment of the Official Zoning Map:

1. Consistency with the goals, policies, and future land use map of the city master plan, including any subarea or corridor studies. If conditions have changed since the city master plan was adopted, the consistency with recent development trends in the area.:

The subject property is zoned I-1, Light Industrial and master planned as Industrial on the Future Land Use Map.

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

a. Future Land Use. The Master Plan designates this site as Industrial on the Future Land Use Map, along with abutting parcels to the south and east. The corresponding Land Use and Policy Goals text desires "to strengthen the environment for light industrial uses and large employers, the City should strive for full occupancy within the City's Industrial Parks."

Utilities and roads on this parcel are part of a previous site development for an Industrial park. The parcel received site plan approval in 1999 with construction of the roads and utilities in 2003. Due to the economic downturn in Michigan at the time, the site was not built beyond the basic infrastructure.

By rezoning this parcel conditionally and developing the parcel with both single and multiple family residential and industrial, the ultimate goal of strengthening the industrial economy in

<a id="p86"></a>
*[Page 86 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=86)* (read by OCR; check the original)

South Lyon may be realized. The anticipated type of industrial development to occur in this area is more in line with the changing economy, logistics, and spending habits—smaller scale industrial speculation buildings that may be immediately occupied.

b. Changing conditions. In 2018, the parcel to the north was conditionally rezoned for the development of an apartment complex, Westlake Apartments. This property was formerly located in Green Oak Township and annexed into the City, with a single-family residential zoning designation, and later conditionally rezoned to multiple-family residential. The proposed multiple family rezoning portion is consistent with, and will be an extension of, Westlake Apartments multiple family residential development.

c. Compatible Use. This conditional rezoning limits the 24.40 +/- acre portion of the property to be developed as multiple family residential units, 1.63+/- acres as single family residential lots, while the remaining 8.14+/- acre portion of the parcel will develop as low-rise industrial development. The City has the ability to permit a use that does not match the Future Land Use designation when it determines that the conditions proposed as part of the rezoning make the requested use compatible with surrounding land uses. The condition offered by the applicant does not affect the land use compatibility.

### 2. Compatibility of the site's physical, geological, hydrological, and other environmental features with the potential uses allowed in the proposed zoning district

The parcel is located adjacent to existing industrial sites as well as in close proximity to both single family and multiple family residential. The site contains over 9 acres of wetlands, however, is large enough to accommodate single and multiple family residential as well as industrial development. The wooded and wet area in the northeast area of the property should not limit the ability to develop the site.

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

<a id="p87"></a>
*[Page 87 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=87)* (read by OCR; check the original)

As a condition of rezoning, this property cannot develop into any other permitted or special land use other than the uses identified in the Conditional Rezoning Agreement and in a manner consistent with the Preliminary Site Plan.

### 3. Evidence the applicant cannot receive a reasonable return on investment through developing the property with one of the uses permitted under the current zoning

The applicant has provided a Market Study and analysis of current real estate trends and projections to support a reasonable return on investment as a residential property and refute a reasonable rate of return on large industrial projects. From the time period the property obtained site plan review until recently, the community, State, and Country endured an economic downturn. Michigan was especially affected by rising unemployment, property value decline and foreclosures, and population loss. Large scale industrial development at this location – on an unpaved, load limited road, Dixboro Road – has not occurred in the 20 years since the initial industrial park approval. The combination of both smaller industrial buildings and multiple family residential fills the residential and industrial use gap.

4. The compatibility of all the potential uses allowed in the proposed zoning district with surrounding uses and zoning in terms of land suitability, impacts on the environment, density, nature of use, traffic impacts, aesthetics, infrastructure, and potential influence on property values.

### a. Uses

Multiple Family Residential. The  $ \underline{\text{multiple family residential uses}} $ allowed in the RM-3 district are consistent with parcels in the vicinity (west of Dixboro Road in Green Oak Township and north of Ten Mile Road at Westlake Apartments and Charleston Park). By limiting the industrial uses to low impact uses, the industrial portion of the property will be compatible with the residential.

The intent of the RM-3, Multiple Residential Zoning District (Section 102-80) is:

○ is designed to provide for planned multiple-family development in limited locations where such land use is not incompatible with single-family residential uses. This district is further provided to accommodate independent and dependent housing facilities.

Single Family Residential. The single family uses allowed in the R-3 district are consistent with the existing single family residential uses on Lake Street.

The intent of the R-3, Single Residential Zoning District (Section 102-70) is:

○ is designed to be the most restrictive of the residential districts and to provide for an environment of predominantly low density, one family detached dwellings.

Industrial. The  $ \underline{\text{industrial development}} $ is consistent with the adjacent properties—Wastewater Treatment Plant and Michigan Seamless Tube—and are intended for research and design, laboratories, recreation, outdoor storage, light manufacturing, assembling, and light fabrication activities, whose external physical effects may be felt to some degree by surrounding properties. Noise from the existing operations (and future) as well as light, dust,

<a id="p88"></a>
*[Page 88 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=88)* (read by OCR; check the original)

and dirt may be a concern with neighboring residential uses, however, as existing industrial uses are existing, residents of the future multiple family residences will be aware of the neighboring conditions before deciding to reside in this area.

The intent of the Industrial Zoning District (Section 102-130):

- Is to primarily accommodate wholesale activities, warehouses, and light industrial operations whose external, physical effects are minimal and in no manner affect in a detrimental way any of the surrounding properties/districts.

Since available industrial land in the city is limited, this district is also intended to accommodate uses that are office- or research-type, along with "business center" buildings that combine office and flexible industrial space.

It is further intended that the processing of raw material for shipment of bulk form, to be used in an industrial operation at another location, is not permitted.

b. Traffic Impacts. An increase in residential traffic and daily trips is anticipated, however, traffic created by heavy weight trucks and other industrial vehicles will be reduced than if developed as an entirely industrially zoned property. The applicant has provided a traffic analysis/impact study for the proposed development. Road modifications are proposed to accommodate the number of vehicles and to reduce traffic conflicts.

c. Aesthetics. The existing multiple family development at Westlake Apartments has high quality materials and architectural details. This proposed development will be a continuation of this same high quality.

d. Infrastructure. The applicant is aware that Dixboro Road lacks the necessary physical improvements to attract industrial development. As such the applicant is proposing to pave Dixboro Road from Lake Street to the southern end of the subject property. This will ensure that industrial vehicles are able to access the site.

The capacity of city infrastructure and services sufficient to accommodate the uses permitted in the requested district without compromising the "health, safety, and welfare" of the city.

The proposed rezoning will increase the demand for public services; however, water and sanitary services have been designed to accommodate additional development (previously designated as only industrial on this property which depending on the use may be substantial). The City Engineers and the DPW should work with the applicant to accurately estimate the demand for water and sanitary services.

e. Use Demand. The apparent demand for the types of uses permitted in the requested zoning district in the city in relation to the amount of land in the city currently zoned to accommodate the demand.

The City of South Lyon land use inventory is rich in residential development. Sixty-seven percent (67%) of the land within the City is zoned and/or used for residential purposes; however, industrially zoned and utilized land in the City accounts for 4.3%. Industrial land is a critical component of the City's land use system and is an important source of jobs, income, and services. Industrial uses, despite perceived challenges of noise, air quality, and truck

<a id="p89"></a>
*[Page 89 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=89)* (read by OCR; check the original)

traffic, are critical to the health and growth of the City. They boost the tax base and provide jobs that enable residents to stay in the South Lyon area. Given the lack of developable land in the City, the City must actively protect its remaining industrial districts.

As the industrial zoned property has remained undeveloped for decades, there are clear obstacles to developing it. Real estate brokers have indicated that the current condition of Dixboro Road (unpaved) presents a challenge for industrial users. As part of this development proposal, the applicant proposes to pave Dixboro Road from Lake Street to the south property line, increasing opportunities for industrial development of that area.

The rezoning shifts will result in a 2% decrease in industrial land and a 1.6% increase in multiple family residential.

<table><tr><td>Use</td><td>Acres</td><td>Percent of Total Land Area</td><td>Percent With Conditional Rezoned Development</td></tr><tr><td>Single Family</td><td>1186</td><td>59.1</td><td>59.1</td></tr><tr><td>Multiple Family</td><td>162</td><td>8</td><td>9.6</td></tr><tr><td>Commercial</td><td>87</td><td>4.3</td><td>4.3</td></tr><tr><td>Industrial</td><td>87</td><td>4.3</td><td>2.8</td></tr><tr><td>Governmental</td><td>256</td><td>12.8</td><td>12.8</td></tr><tr><td>Parks and Open Space</td><td>154</td><td>7.7</td><td>7.7</td></tr><tr><td>Transportation</td><td>56</td><td>2.8</td><td>2.8</td></tr><tr><td>Undeveloped</td><td>18</td><td>.9</td><td>.9</td></tr></table>

f. Most Appropriate Zoning. Where a rezoning is reasonable given the above criteria, a determination shall be made that the requested zoning district is more appropriate than another district or amending the list of permitted or special land uses within a district.

The City's master plan has a goal to "provide housing to match the varied needs and income levels of the present and future population." Currently multiple family residential comprise eight percent (8%) of the total existing land use in the City, and twelve percent (12%) of all residential. The proposed housing works toward the goal of providing high quality housing for a diverse population with varying incomes, community gathering spaces, and safe, convenient routes and access.

A Master Plan goal for Industrial land is “to strive for full occupancy within the City’s Industrial Parks.” The property to the south of our subject site is a vacant industrial park—with infrastructure in place and waiting for build out and tenancy. Designating a portion of the site for low intensity industrial uses will further the goal for industrial growth.

This conditional rezoning limits the property to be developed as multiple-family residential and industrial development and excludes all other permitted uses. The City has the ability to

<a id="p90"></a>
*[Page 90 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=90)* (read by OCR; check the original)

permit a use that does not match the Future Land Use designation when it determines that the conditions proposed make the requested use compatible with surrounding land uses.

Following a public hearing for a proposed zoning amendment, the Planning Commission makes a recommendation to the City Council based upon the criteria listed in Section 102-213, Criteria for Amendment of the Official Zoning Map (rezoning).

In addition, following a public hearing to consider a Conditional Rezoning, the Planning Commission shall consider and address in written findings whether a proposed conditional rezoning:

1. Is consistent with the intent of this article.

2. Bears a reasonable and rational connection and/or benefit to the property being proposed for rezoning.

3. Is necessary to ensure that the property develops in such a way that protects the surrounding neighborhood.

4. Leads to a better development than would have been likely if the property had been rezoned without a conditional rezoning, or if the property were left to develop under the existing zoning classification.

5. Is clearly in the public interest.

## Part 2:

Preliminary Site Plan Review

A Conditional Rezoning ties a specific preliminary site plan/conceptual plan to the rezoning of the property. No other use or site plan development may occur on a conditionally rezoned parcel other than that shown in the Preliminary Site Plan, approved as part of the Conditional Rezoning Agreement.

The Preliminary Site Plan approval provides general acceptance of the road layout, lot (unit) sizes, open space, preliminary landscaping, and housing designs. After a public hearing has been held for the Conditional Rezoning and Preliminary Plan review, a recommendation from the Planning Commission to the City Council is made. If the Conditional Rezoning and corresponding Preliminary Site Plan are approved by the City Council, the applicant will return to the Planning Commission for Final Site Plan review and approval. All of the requirements for site plan submissions in Article IV of the Zoning Ordinance must be complied with at that time.

As a part of the Conditional Rezoning Review, we have reviewed the Preliminary Site Plan for development of both multiple family residential and industrial, to be built in three (3) phases (and as an extension of the existing West Lake Apartments, identified as Phase 1 on the site plan).

The opinions in this report are based on a review of the site plan submitted by the applicant and conformance to City plans and ordinance standards. Please note that the applicant and their design professionals shall be responsible for the accuracy and validity of information presented with the application and on the site plan.

## PRELIMINARY SITE PLAN REVIEW COMMENTS

1. Overall Site Development. The applicant is proposing to conditionally rezone 24.40+/- acres of the industrially zoned parcel to RM-3 to develop 252 new multiple family apartment units and rezone 1.63+/- acres to R-3 to develop three (3) single family residential lots fronting on Lake Street, east of Dixboro, and use the remaining 8.14+/- acres to develop up to 63,742 square-feet of industrial. The multiple family residential use will be accessed via two (2) curb cuts on Dixboro and a divided entrance on Lake Street (constructed at later phases); the single family lots will be accessed via curb cuts/private driveways along Lake Street; and the industrial use is accessed via a separate curb cut on Dixboro that accesses the industrial portion only.

For the residential component, the applicant is additionally proposing 28 garages, 4 car charging stations, a 3,000 square foot club house, covered patio, and outdoor pool, walking paths, gazebos, community garden, pickle ball courts, bike racks and a dog park. If approved as envisioned, the Westlake project will include three (3) phases of residential development each with 84 units.

a. Residential Density. The applicant is proposing 252 units over 24.40 $ \pm $ acres which equals  $ \sim $10 units per acre. With a site area over 1 million square feet and a total of 584 bedrooms, the site area per bedroom is approximately 1,949 square feet per bedroom. Unit sizes are between 600 and 664 square feet for a one (1) bedroom unit; 768 to 947 square feet for a

<a id="p92"></a>
*[Page 92 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=92)* (read by OCR; check the original)

two (2) bedroom unit; and 936 square feet for a three (3) bedroom unit. Three (3) R-3, single family outlets on West Lake are proposed.

b. Access and Circulation. Twenty-seven (27) foot drive aisles circulate the site and provide access to all phases and buildings. The residential use will be accessed via two (2) curb cuts on Dixboro and a divided entrance on Lake Street (constructed at a later phase). The relocation of the entrance off of Lake Street is to minimize the number of traffic conflicts at Dixboro and Lake (10 Mile). Funneling residential traffic to the main boulevard entrance will reduce the number of residential vehicles turning from Dixboro. The industrial use is accessed via a separate curb cut on Dixboro- with a horseshow style drive- accessing the industrial portion only. There is no vehicular connection between the residential and the industrial uses.

Per the Zoning Ordinance, the parking requirements for the multiple family residential total 596 spaces.  $ \underline{\text{This amount of parking has been found to be excessive based on the existing}} $
