## New Business

a.  $ \underline{\text{Public Hearing}} $: SPR#22-01, 432 Washington Avenue. The applicant is seeking preliminary PUD site plan approval for the development of a 3-story, 53-Unit Multiple-Family residential building. The property identification number is 21-30-234-006.

b.  $ \underline{\text{Consider Preliminary PUD Site Plan}} $: SPR #22-01, 432 Washington Avenue. The applicant is seeking preliminary PUD site plan approval for the development of a 3-story, 53-unit multiple-family residential building. The property identification number is 21-30-234-006

Smith states that the Planning Commission saw this project before them in April and May of 2022, when it came for PUD preliminary site plan approval. She states that this is a proposed redevelopment of the Housing Commission property to construct a 53 unit, multiple-family, senior affordable housing development. At the May 2022 meeting, the Planning Commission recommended approval of the preliminary PUD site plan to City Council.

Following that meeting, the City identified some issues with the Housing Commission and that it had not been properly constituted and the members not properly appointment per City Ordinance. Because of this, the application was put on hold until the Commission was properly put together. The Housing Commission is now properly formed and functioning, and the project has picked up again.

Smith state that the developer has presented the same plan that the Commission saw last year to the Housing Commission, who provided their approval and support of the plan moving forward at their September 27, 2023 meeting.

She added, when the project picked back up, the City Attorney and staff worked together to determine the proper next steps in the process. In evaluating the steps up to this point, it was agreed upon that procedurally it should return to the Planning Commission for PUD preliminary site plan to clean up some legal procedural items. This includes both having Housing Commission approval, and the fact that the PUD Overlay should have been reviewed rather than a rezoning to a PUD district.

She confirms that the plans in front of the Commissioners are the same plans that were reviewed and recommended for approval in May 2022. While the plans are the same, we did ask for a new application from the applicant, as well as an updated PUD narrative which has been signed by both the developer and the executive director of the Housing Commission giving their support.

She states that they did update the review letter, but again this is mostly procedural items and terminology rather than site plan review comments changing. The other review letters are the same as last year. We also have included a draft PUD Agreement for the project for your review as well, which was in your packets.

<a id="p240"></a>
*[Page 240 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=240)* (read by OCR; check the original)

She states that she won't run through our whole report in detail, but is happy to answer specific questions. Adding that she wants to remind the Commission that any motion tonight is two separate motions – one for the PUD Overlay designation and one for the PUD preliminary site plan. If recommended for approval, the request would move on to City Council and if approved by Council, would return to the Planning Commission for final site plan approval.

Smith states that they are recommending that the Planning Commission recommend approval of the PUD Overlay designation and the PUD preliminary site plan for Washington Manor Senior Apartments. Per the City Attorney's recommendation, we have a print out of our updated recommendation at your table tonight. The added text is in red. They are as follows:

Based upon the above review,  $ \underline{\text{we recommend that the Planning Commission recommend}} $ approval to City Council for the Planned Unit Development (PUD) Overlay designation for  $ \underline{\text{Washington Manor Senior Apartments}} $ based upon the proposed development meeting the minimum Planned Unit Development Overlay standards by demonstrating:

1. A complementary mixture of housing types and uses:

2. Increased availability of much needed affordable housing for low-to-moderate income senior residents;

3. Availability of common open space for passive or active recreational use, including 4,500 square feet of fully-accessible community space, outdoor patios and direct access to McHattie Park and South Lyon walking trails;

4. A reduction in traffic and associated community impacts than traditional multiple family housing developments; and

5. The goals and objectives of the City of South Lyon Master Plan will be met.

We further recommend that the Planning Commission recommend approval to City Council for the Planned Unit Development (PUD) Overlay Preliminary Site Plan for Washington Manor Senior Apartments subject to the City granting modification waivers for the following, based upon a finding that the deviations shall result in a higher quality of development than would be possible using conventional zoning standards:

1. a waiver to allow a density of 35 units per acre;

2. a waiver to allow a building height of 3 stories and 37 feet;

3. a waiver to permit a parking encroachment of 14 feet into the northern greenbelt along West McHattie;

4. a waiver to allow a front yard encroachment of 10 feet to permit a stair well at the southeast corner of the building; and

5. a waiver to permit a dumpster enclosure in the front yard.

This recommendation of approval is subject to the condition that an updated site plan with a corrected and complete table of modifications, meeting Section 102-154, be provided to City Council.

c. Public Hearing: RZ #23-01. Westlake Conditional Rezoning & Preliminary Site Plan. The applicant is proposing to conditionally rezone a portion of a 34.17-acre parcel, #21-30-126-003, to RM-3 Multiple-Family Residential (24.40 acres) and a portion to R-3 Single Family Residential (1.63 acres) to develop 252 multiple-family residential units and 3 single-family residential lots. d.  $ \underline{\text{Consider Conditional Rezoning & Preliminary Site Plan}} $: RZ #23-01, Westland

Conditional Rezoning & Preliminary Site Plan. The applicant is proposing to

conditionally rezone a portion of a 34-17-acre parcel, #21-30-126-003, to RM-3

Multiple-Family Residential (24.40 acres) and a portion to R-3 Single-Family

Residential (1.63 acres) to develop 252 multiple-family residential units and 3 single-family residential lots. The remaining 8.14 acres will remain I-1, Light Industrial and is

proposed to be developed with approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial building space.

## Public Hearing – 7:22 p

Smith states that the Westlake project that was before the Planning Commission on September 28, 2023, where the Commission held a public hearing and made a positive recommendation to the City Council on the conditional rezoning request for a Planned Unit Development to include multiple-family, single-family, and industrial land uses at the corner of Lake Street and Dixboro. As we were preparing the project to go to City Council, the City Attorney, City Manager, and City Planners re-evaluated the request and agreed that the conditional rezoning should be for a portion of the property to be rezoned to RM-3 Multiple-Family and R-3 Single-Family, rather than conditionally rezone the whole property to PUD. So the request before you tonight takes out the PUD element.

Since then, the applicant has submitted a revised conditional rezoning application for the property along with a preliminary site plan, a written supplement, preliminary architectural drawings and a preliminary landscape plan, which has attached to it certain conditions offered by the applicant. The request is rezone a portion – roughly 24.4 acres – to RM-3 multiple-family residential, a portion – roughly 1.63 acres – to R-3 single-family residential, and the remaining 8.14 acres to remain 1.1 light industrial. As a reminder on the conditional rezoning, the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act allows for the conditional rezoning of land and if approved, it is tied to a specific use and the conditions offered by the applicant.

The plans have not changed in terms of layout, circulation, or other details. Simply, the rezoning request and legal descriptions have been updated to reflect the elimination of the PUD and just show the conditional rezoning of portions of the property. The task before the Planning Commission tonight is to address the revised application, hold the public hearing, and send a revised recommendation to City Council. This is a legal and procedural matter rather than a detailed re-evaluation of the site plans.

<a id="p244"></a>
*[Page 244 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=244)* (read by OCR; check the original)

There is also an updated draft of the conditional rezoning agreement in your packets for your review that the City Attorney and applicant have been working on together diligently.

You should have an updated review letter from us that reflected the changes with the new application, as well as other review letters that have not changed and application materials such as the market study and traffic impact study. These too have not changed. I will note that our letter and recommendation had some slight revisions at the attorney's recommendation – you should all have a copy of this at your table tonight and have received it electronically. These are predominantly wording changes to make the wording consistent with that of a conditional rezoning request. The only changes in the updated letter are the change of the word "Deviation" to "Condition," and the correction of number of apartments that was miswritten. These changes are highlighted in yellow for your convenience.

I will not run through our reports in detail as we did that at the last meeting, but I will go over the updated recommendations and am happy to answer any specific questions. For a conditional rezoning, there are two separate motions that are required — one on the conditional rezoning request and one on the preliminary site plan to which it is tied. As a reminder, these are recommendations to City Council

## Bob Langan, Principle of Elevate Properties, LLC, 128 North Center Street, Northville, Michigan

Langan states that he is back to go over their presentation that they originally presented at the 9-28-2023 Planning Commission Meeting. The one change that he wanted to point out is the existing project on the corner, the 72 units that we currently own, we originally included that in the PUD, thinking that one thing would be beneficial, but it is actually easier to leave it out.

Langan refers to the Site Plan and adds that they are proposing 252 new units, 1, 2 and 3 bedroom market rate apartments, along with approximately 63,000 square feet of light industrial, and 3 single family residential homes along Lake Street. He adds that there are some significant public improvements that is a condition of this, which include paving a portion of Dixboro Road, proposing to build a new entrance on Lake Street which will enhance public and traffic safety. Another improvement that they are offering is paving non-motorized pathways connecting to the Huron Valley Trail and connecting to the sidewalks in the City. Langan refers to the aerial view slide in his presentation.

Langan goes on to point out the single row of garages and adds that some of these will be converted in to multiple storage space opportunities for the residents. He then goes over the images for the elevations, preserving the street appeal. He also shows the Club House and how it was enhanced above the elevations of the buildings. He adds that the site amenities include an outdoor pool, a 24/7 fitness facility, pickle ball courts, a dog park, and they are building common areas to encourage gathering. They will also include a community garden, bike racks and they are proposing 6-8 EV charging stations. He states that they do have a lot of open spaces and they are preserving a lot of the trees.

Langan goes on to point out the Industrial area and how they are using a modular concept, giving them the ability to change the façade. He adds that they are trying to keep it flexible and they believe that there may be some more business oriented users too. Including dance studios, doggy daycare, a gym, or some of these business uses that are becoming popular.

<a id="p245"></a>
*[Page 245 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=245)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Langan points out that the next slide is new and states that they listened to some of the comments that were made from the last meeting. He stated that they thought it would be important to address the environmental impact of this development. He goes on to say that they do have a wetland impact on this site. It is born only of the observation that the location of the entrance on Lake Street is superior for public and traffic safety. Fortunately, that lines up with Charleston Park at the narrowest point of the wetlands on the entire site. He goes on to say that they are proposing to cross 40 feet of wetlands using culverts and doing minimal disturbances to the wetlands. There is no interruption of water flow or wetland critters or anything between the wetlands. Langan states that EGLE has been there informally to validate whether or not this is a reasonable proposition. EGLE agrees informally, but it is subject to EGLE and other governmental approval. If this were to be denied, they would simply leave the existing entrance where it is. Langan goes on to state that they have also done a parking study, which allows them to preserve 50% of the woodlands on the property. He goes on to say that this site was developed 25 years ago, and most of the woodlands that they are proposing to get rid of, have already been gotten rid of 25 years ago. It is now just the pioneer trees that have come up in the last 25 years. The quality trees that are on the site and mostly surrounding the wetlands are being preserved. Langan goes on to discuss the significance of the non-motorized pathways and ads that their multi-family proposal has lot coverage at less than half of what it would be if this were developed as strictly industrial. Langan adds that this site is all electrical, there is no gas, no combustible gas and no fumes. The building program is using enhanced energy seal on all the buildings, and we beef up sound protection. Extra money will be spent on the windows and we want to make it known that we are conscious of the impact that we make.

Chair Lanam verifies with Langan, for Phase II, the number of residential units and that the balance will be Industrial. Langan adds that they are committed to Phase I, and there is not enough market data to commit to any Industrial use. He states that if and when we have success with the first building they will immediately start the next Phase, and we are committed to it.

## Clif Seiber, Civil Engineer, Seiber Keast Engineering, 100 Maincentre, Suite 10, Northville, Michigan

Langan confirms that the will need the EGLE permit before they can start Phase II. Lanam questions the process for the wetlands and moving the entrance. Seiber states that the only wetland crossing will be easterly of Phase I, there is 280 feet of boardwalk that will be installed there and this will be elevated above the wetlands. In reference to the roadway, EGLE will require a 2 to 1 mitigation. He goes on to say that the fill would be placed and then the roadway would be constructed on top of it.

Lanam questions if EGLE does not approve this, will it have to come back to Planning. City Attorney Hamameh states that he would probably not be getting EGLE approval before final approval of the site plan. So, he would have to come back for an amendment to the conditional rezoning agreement and the plan if anything deviates from what has been approved. She adds, coming back to Planning will depend on the extent of the change. Smith adds that any approval from Planning Commission, Council, will be conditional upon if they got the agency approval for this plan. Lanam verifies that in the event they do not move the entrance way, they will still need to get the Fire Marshalls approval.

Lanam questions if any of the existing roads and utilities are being retained. Langan states that the industrial access road that terminates in the cul-de-sac is the current road. The Dixboro Road entrance that we are moving, we will be saving all that we can. The pavement will come out. Lanam adds that assuming that we get to this point, when you come back, we will need the final materials. Lanam also questions the 3 single family dwellings that will be going up. Langan states that they will go up right away and they will be designed to be compatible to the City of South Lyon. Smith adds that just the lots

are approved for the conditional rezoning. In terms of the single family homes, they do not normally come to the Planning Commission, they will go on to the Building Department. Commissioner Joseph asks Langan to clarify the difference between Phase I and Phase II. He also asks what the timetable is for Phase II and Phase III. Commissioner Joseph confirms that they will come back with the details of their buildings.

Public Comments —

## Judy Kate, 25217 Potomac, South Lyon, Michigan

Kate questions if the new buildings are going to resemble the current buildings and if the current buildings are going to be incorporated in to this development. To which Langan states they are.

## Suzann Martin, 602 West Lake Street, South Lyon, Michigan

Martin states that this development proposes to build a Boulevard through and over the 9 acre rare wetlands. The wetlands are rare because only 5% of the United States wetlands remain. These wetlands, if undisturbed, can continue to exist as a natural water feature on the property. The wetlands can bring value to their development by making the homes, apartments and the industrial buildings more desirable, due to the water features and preservation efforts. The wetlands uptake the water from storms that we are experiencing more often and more intensely. Through the storm drains in the City and those wetlands water runs directly to surrounding water ways such as the Huron River, which other communities use for their drinking water. It is essential that the water runoff is pure. This is one reason that the City of South Lyon has a storm water Management Planning Program under the Department of Public Works which she has been involved since 2005. Martin states that she would appreciate very much if the Commission would ask the developer to re-draw the blue prints and to not have an access road through and over the wetlands. Thank you.

### Ryan Cottingim, 601 S. Lafayette, South Lyon, Michigan

Cottingham states that he is also here to speak about the wetlands. He states that he feels that the whole project can be done without the Boulevard, it is not necessary. He adds that they state that it is a safety concern until EGLE denies it and then it is not a safety concern and that they would move forward and that there would be enough access for everyone to go safely. It doesn't make sense and they are taking something that has been a part of this community that has made this community better. It's going to rip it apart and it will never be the same. They are going to destroy it just to put it back. But it is not going to be put back in to ours, it will go to a bank in another community. He has been driving by those since he was a kid, by the wetland area, his dad has been driving by that area since he was a kid. He states that we should be proud of that and we can preserve that. He is not against the project, just the Boulevard.

<a id="p247"></a>
*[Page 247 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=247)* (read by OCR; check the original)

The Boulevard does not need to happen. He states that the last time he was here speaking about this project, he was told that the Planning Commission really has no say in this wetland. They can't stop this from happening, but they can. They can vote this down, you can stop this change in zoning or you can add waivers. You can vote for this without this and you can save the wetland. So if you vote yes for this, you will be voting yes to destroy our wetlands that have been a part of our community for a long time. He hopes that this is taken in to consideration.

Smith explains the criteria for amendment of the zoning map, the first one is consistency with goals of the Master Plan and the Future Land Use map. The subject property is zoned I-1 and is master planned as Industrial. A goal of the master plan for industrial is to strengthen the environment for light industrial uses. Rezoning the property conditionally and having mixed-use of residential and industrial may help realize the goal of strengthening the industrial economy. It's proposed to be lower impact industrial uses and the property has been zoned industrial but sat empty for a number of years. In terms of changing conditions, the property to the north was conditionally rezoned for Westlake Apartments from single-family residential to multiple-family residential. The proposed multiple-family portion of this proposal is consistent with and an extension of that development.

The second criteria is compatibility with the site's physical, geographic, and environmental features and compatibility with potential uses allowed in the district. The site does contain over 9 acres of wetlands, but is large enough to accommodate the residential and industrial development while mostly staying away from those wetlands. As a condition of the rezoning, the property cannot develop with uses other than those identified in the agreement and is tied to the site plan you're looking at.

The third criteria is that the applicant can't receive a reasonable return on investment with uses permitted under the current zoning. The applicant provided a market study analyzing current real estate trends which support reasonable return on investment for residential use and refute a reasonable return on large industrial projects. As noted, large scale industrial development has not occurred in the last twenty years since the industrial park was initially proposed. There are challenges to develop that site as fully industrial including the unpaved Dixboro Road.

The fourth is compatibility of potential uses with surrounding uses and zoning. The multiple-family is consistent with parcels in the vicinity of the property. By limiting the industrial uses to lower impact industrial the industrial portion is more compatible with multiple-family residential. The single-family residential uses proposed to be rezoned to R-3 are consistent with existing single-family uses along Lake Street. Industrial uses are also consistent with adjacent industrial properties, including Michigan Seamless Tube and the Water Plant. Proposed uses will be lower impact than existing industrial in the area. In terms of traffic impacts, naturally an increase in trips is anticipated but the impacts compared to the alternative full industrial zoning are lesser than what would be created by heavy industrial truck traffic. The applicant provided a traffic impact analysis and has also proposed some road modifications to mitigate traffic conflicts. In terms of aesthetics, the existing Westlake Apartments development uses high-quality materials and this is proposed to be a continuation of that quality. In terms of infrastructure and public services, they are proposing to pave Dixboro to the southern end of the property. Water and sanitary sewer have been designed to accommodate development of that property; the City Engineers and DPW will work with the applicant to properly determine the demands on those services.

In terms of use demand, the current land use inventory of the City is rich in residential development and only about 4.3% is zoned for industrial. Given the lack of undeveloped land in the City, the City has to protect the remaining industrial property that it has. As this property has been zoned industrial and has been undeveloped for many years, there are clear obstacles such as the unpaved road. We believe this

<a id="p248"></a>
*[Page 248 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=248)* (read by OCR; check the original)

rezoning would allow some of this industrial land to be preserved and supported by the addition of the residential.

The next criteria is most appropriate zoning and a determination that the requested district is more appropriate than the current. The City's Master Plan has a goal to provide housing matching the needs and income levels of the population; the proposed housing in this development works towards providing quality housing for a diverse population, as well as community gathering spaces, safe pedestrian routes, and other benefits. The master plan also has the goal to have full occupancy of the City's industrial parks. Occupying the lower-intensity industrial spaces in the proposed development help to support this goal. The conditional rezoning limits the property to be used to these uses and excludes other uses; they are tied to this plan. The conditional rezoning also allows the city to permit uses that doesn't match the future land use designation when the proposed uses are compatible with the surrounding area.

I'm going to do a high level review of the site plan items.

The conditional rezoning is tied to this plan; any changes would have to be approved as an amendment. The conditional rezoning is proposing about 24 acres to RM-3 to accommodate 252 new multiple-family units, the three single-family lots are about 1.63 acres, and the remaining 8.14 acres will be industrial and developed with about 64,000 square feet of industrial.

To touch on parking – per the zoning ordinance for the multiple family portion, 596 parking spots would be required. They are proposing 430 spots based upon the traffic study that was prepared and based on the existing development, which was built with the required amount of parking, has been found to be excessive. Based on those two elements, the number of spots is proposed to be reduced by 166 spaces. If the Planning Commission agrees with the traffic study and determines that this reduction in parking is warranted, that is a condition of the rezoning. The condition offered by the applicant would be accepted. For the industrial portion, without knowing what the end users will be, it is difficult to determine the required amount as it varies a lot based on uses permitted. For example, a warehousing use is very different in terms of required parking than a gym use, both of which are are industrial uses. They are proposing 223 parking spots. Without having known users at this time, they are proposing a fairly large increase over the range of industrial uses. This would also require acceptance of the condition for that increase in parking. Planning Commission could also authorize the planner or engineer to administratively evaluate and approve the required parking as each building is built out or when a site plan is submitted for each specific configuration of remaining industrial. If the remaining approved amount of parking far exceeds the demand, the remaining parking can be deferred on as-built plans and be constructed if there is a demonstrated need for it.

To discuss natural features, they did a woodland survey. 50% of the existing trees will be preserved and over 627 inches DBH will be replaced throughout the site. This meets our standards there is a 9-acre EGLE-regulated wetland on the property. They are required to have permits for any work within the wetland area from EGLE and they are maintaining a 50-foot setback from the wetlands.

In terms of landscaping, there is technically a buffer that would be required between the industrial and the multiple-family residential uses. That is another condition being offered – since this is a mixed-use development, that buffer would not be provided. There is no vehicular access between the two, they are separate driveways. They are providing street trees and plantings.

They are proposing a continuation of the existing development in terms of architecture and use of durable exterior building materials. The industrial elevations were provided and meet standards. As

<a id="p249"></a>
*[Page 249 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=249)* (read by OCR; check the original)

noted, the single-family lots will be subject to our residential infill housing standards that regulate the character of infill housing.

There is a table of conditions of the rezoning in the letter that I will summarize. The first is minimum floor area; the R-3 district has a minimum 1,000 sq ft, 4-bedroom apartment units, they are proposing 1-bedroom, 600 sq ft to 3-bedroom, 936 sq ft apartments. As discussed, the parking for multiple-family and industrial. The next is the 35-foot greenbelt requirement to connect and develop the multiple-family as extension of the existing apartments, a greenbelt separating land uses and access drives is not warranted. That is the same for the buffer requirement residential and industrial, as it is a mixed-use development. You should have updated recommendations. The Planning Commission will be making two separate motions. The first one is for the conditional rezoning:

Given the above analysis, we recommend approval of the conditional rezoning for a portion of parcel # 21-301-26-003, from I-1, Light Industrial, to RM-3. Multiple Family Residential and R-3, Single Family Residential, to permit 252 multiple family residential units, 3 single family lots, and approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial development on the remaining industrial portion of the parcel based upon the following reasons:

1. Although the industrial master plan designation is not entirely consistent with the proposed residential and industrial development properties with the same planned future use designation (Industrial) are in close proximity to the subject parcel;

2. The proposed rezoning bears a reasonable and rational connection and/or benefit to the property being proposed for rezoning-- adjacency to industrial and residential property; an appropriate transition between abutting industrial, multiple family and single family residential; and is necessary to ensure that the property develops in such a way that protects the surrounding neighborhoods.

3. The proposed development includes community gathering spaces, safe sidewalks, and improvement to a portion of Dixboro Road.

4. The industrial property has remained vacant for two (2) decades despite the installation of utilities; the proposed mixed use, paving of Dixboro Road, and construction of industrial speculation buildings will encourage immediate occupancy of light industrial and commercial uses;

5. The site's physical and other environmental features are compatible with the proposed use; the residential development surrounding and adjacent to the wetlands protects the wetland from potential industrial impacts and is more conducive than industrial development;

6. The proposed use is compatible with surrounding uses and zoning in terms of land suitability, impacts on the environment, traffic volumes, aesthetics, infrastructure, and addressing a community need;

7. The street system is capable of safely and efficiently accommodating the limited expected traffic volumes generated by the proposed use; and

8. The capacity of public utilities and services is sufficient to accommodate the proposed use without compromising the city's health, safety, and welfare.

9. The Conditional Rezoning is conditioned upon the signing and execution of the Conditional Rezoning Agreement (Exhibit A).

Second, as the conditional rezoning is tied to a site plan, we are also recommending approval of the Conditional Rezoning Preliminary Site Plan, dated November 8, 2023, conditioned upon:

November 15, 2023

Planning Commission

City of South Lyon

335 South Warren Street

South Lyon, Michigan 48178

## Subject:

REVISED - Westlake Conditional Rezoning Review Light Industrial I-1, to Multiple Family, RM-3 and Single Family Residential, R-3. Approximately 34.42 $ \pm $/- acre parcel located on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Lake Street (Ten Mile), Parcel #21-30-126-003.

Dear Planning Commissioners:

Elevate Land Holdings LLC, a Delaware Limited Liability Company, has petitioned the City and requested a Conditional Rezoning of a portion of a 34.17+/-acre parcel of industrially zoned land on Dixboro Road, parcel # 21-30-126-003, to allow 252 new multiple family residential units, 3 single family residential lots and the remaining portion of the parcel be developed with approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial development. To allow for this, the request is to rezone 24.40+/- acres to RM-3, Multiple-Family Residential, 1.63+/- acres to R-3, Single Family Residential, and the remaining 8.14+/- acres to remain I-1, Light Industrial. The request is pursuant to M.C.L. 125.3405.

The Michigan Zoning Enabling statute allows for the conditional rezoning of land offering another tool for property owners seeking the ability to use their property in a way other than what is allowed by current zoning. If approved, a conditional rezoning ties the use and any development of a property to specific conditions offered by the property owner/applicant.

M.C.L 125.3405 Use and development of land as condition to rezoning.

Sec. 405. (1) An owner of land may voluntarily offer in writing, and the local unit of government may approve, certain use and development of the land as a condition to a rezoning of the land or an amendment to a zoning map.

In approving the conditions under subsection (1), the local unit of government may establish a time period during which the conditions apply to the land. Except for an extension under subsection (4), if the conditions are not satisfied within the time specified under this subsection, the land shall revert to its former zoning classification.

The local government shall not add to or alter the conditions approved under subsection (1) during the time period specified under subsection (2) of this section.

The time period specified under subsection (2) may be extended upon the application of the landowner and approval of the local unit of government.

<a id="p254"></a>
*[Page 254 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=254)* (read by OCR; check the original)

A local unit of government shall not require a landowner to offer conditions as a requirement for rezoning. The lack of an offer under subsection (1) shall not otherwise affect a landowner's rights under this act, the ordinances of the local unit of government, or any other laws of this state.

## CONDITIONAL REZONING PROCESS

The process to review and approve a Conditional Rezoning has four (4) stages:

Step 1: Public

Hearing, PC Review

and

Recommendation

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

## Step 1:

Public Hearing, Conditional Rezoning Review, Preliminary Site Plan/Conceptual Plan Review, and Conditional Rezoning Agreement draft recommendation from the Planning Commission to the City Council

Step 2: Council's first reading of the Conditional Rezoning, Preliminary site plan/conceptual plan approval and Conditional Rezoning Agreement

Step 3: Council's second reading of the Conditional Rezoning and approval of Conditional Rezoning Agreement.

Step 4: Final Site plan review and approval by the Planning Commission.

This review letter will first address the rezoning portion of the request and then the development and site plan review that accompanies the rezoning.

The applicant is requesting to rezone a portion of the 34.17-acre parcel, parcel # 21-301-26-003, l-1, Light Industrial, to RM-3, Multiple Family and R-3, Single Family Residential. The application submittal for the Conditional Rezoning has an accompanying Preliminary Site Plan and Conditional Rezoning Agreement.

<a id="p255"></a>
*[Page 255 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=255)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## Previous Rezoning attempts for parcel #21-301-26-003:

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

This property was rezoned from Residential to Industrial in 1989 at the request of the previous property owner. A site plan for a light industrial park with 11 units, commonly known as West End Industrial, was approved in 1999 and construction on the park started in 2003. The utilities were installed, and the road constructed, however, no further development occurred, and the site sat dormant. In 2016, the property owner requested that the property be rezoned back to residential use, from 1, Light Industrial, to RM-1, Multiple Family Residential. The Planning Commission recommended denial of the request, based on the following: the proposed rezoning is not consistent with the Master Plan; there is a need to retain the City's limited industrial land; and the site is capable of accommodating the range of uses permitted in the I-1 District. The City Council unanimously denied the application in 2017. Elevate Properties has now requested a Conditional Rezoning to develop the property with both multiple family residential and industrial.

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

<a id="p256"></a>
*[Page 256 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=256)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## CONDITIONAL REZONING REVIEW AND ANALYSIS

The applicant is proposing to change the zoning of a portion of parcel 21-30-126-003 from Light Industrial, I-1, to Multiple Family Residential, RM-3, and Single Family Residential, R-3, as a Conditional Rezoning under Section 405 of the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act (Act 110 of 2006). A Preliminary Site Plan is required to illustrate the proposed development and show that ordinance requirements can be met. Under Section 405 of the Zoning Enabling Act, a petitioner may voluntarily offer in writing, and the City may approve, certain uses and development of property as a condition to rezoning land. The conditions proposed by a petitioner, as part of a conditional rezoning, are supposed to result in recognizable and material benefits to the City that would unlikely be achieved otherwise under the site's existing zoning.

## As part of this conditional rezoning request, the petitioner is voluntarily offering the following:

1. Phased development of a Mixed-Use Development consisting of:

a. 24.40 $ \pm $ acres of multiple family residential, 1.63 $ \pm $ acres of single family residential, and 8.14 $ \pm $ acres of light industrial;

b. 252 new multiple residential units, consisting of 1-, 2-, and 3-bedroom residential units;

c. 3 single family residential detached dwelling units;

d. Approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial building space on remaining industrially zoned property.

2. Development of Phase 1 portion of the Industrial and Phase 1 Multiple Family Residential concurrently;

3. Constructed as part of Phase 1 Industrial and Phase 1 Multiple Family Residential development, paving of 1,088 lineal feet of Dixboro Road from Lake Street to the southerly end of the property.

4. At the time of Dixboro Road paving, the pedestrian pathway ultimately connecting to Huron Valley Trail, measuring 1,088 lineal feet along Dixboro Rd, will be installed from Lake Street to  $ \underline{\text{the southerly end of the property line}} $;

5. As part of Phase 1 development, a payment of $36,200 to the City of South Lyon will be made for the future construction of the remaining 724 lineal feet from southerly property line to Huron Valley Trail.

6. As part of Phase 2 Multiple Family Residential development, a pedestrian pathway (connecting to the pedestrian pathway constructed in Phase 1) will be constructed along Lake Street from the corner of Lake Street and Dixboro Road to South Hagadorn Road connecting to the existing sidewalk that leads to downtown.

7. Provided that all necessary permits are secured by the applicable state, county, city or other governmental agency, the existing Lake Street driveway approach serving Westlake Place Apartments (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005) will be removed and relocated during Phase 2 in order to improve public and traffic safety. If an amendment to the final approved site plan is required, it shall be considered a minor revision.

## LOCATION AND DESCRIPTION

The site is surrounded by varied uses – to the north is single-family residence and a multiple-family residential apartment complex, West Lake Apartment, owned by the applicant; to the west are single-family residential homes (in Green Oak Township); industrial to the south (Michigan Seamless Tube and the City's Wastewater Treatment Facility) and east (Michigan Seamless Tube). The property has frontage on both Dixboro Road and Lake Street (Ten Mile Road). Dixboro Road divides the City and Green Oak Township. Dixboro Road is not paved, and while many discussions have indicated that it will be paved, the timing of that construction is unknown. A portion of this parcel was cleared and prepared for industrial development, including installation of utilities and a road. A significant portion of the parcel contains wetlands with topography falling to the north and east.

As this is a rezoning request, consideration should be given to the proposed use(s) as it relates to the surrounding zoning and land uses, existing infrastructure, and most importantly consistency with the Master Plan.

## CONDITIONAL REZONING REVIEW STANDARDS

In reviewing this application, the Planning Commission should consider additional comments from the applicant, relevant factual new information presented at the public hearing, and the following criteria, among other factors they may deem appropriate, in making their findings, decision and corresponding recommendation, based upon Section 102-213-Criteria for Amendment of the Official Zoning Map:

1. Consistency with the goals, policies, and future land use map of the city master plan, including any subarea or corridor studies. If conditions have changed since the city master plan was adopted, the consistency with recent development trends in the area.

The subject property is zoned I-1, Light Industrial and master planned as Industrial on the Future Land Use Map.

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

a. Future Land Use. The Master Plan designates this site as Industrial on the Future Land Use Map, along with abutting parcels to the south and east. The corresponding Land Use and Policy Goals text desires "to strengthen the environment for light industrial uses and large employers, the City should strive for full occupancy within the City's Industrial Parks."

Utilities and roads on this parcel are part of a previous site development for an Industrial park. The parcel received site plan approval in 1999 with construction of the roads and utilities in 2003. Due to the economic downturn in Michigan at the time, the site was not built beyond the basic infrastructure.

By rezoning this parcel conditionally and developing the parcel with both single and multiple family residential and industrial, the ultimate goal of strengthening the industrial economy in

<a id="p262"></a>
*[Page 262 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=262)* (read by OCR; check the original)

South Lyon may be realized. The anticipated type of industrial development to occur in this area is more in line with the changing economy, logistics, and spending habits—smaller scale industrial speculation buildings that may be immediately occupied.

b. Changing conditions. In 2018, the parcel to the north was conditionally rezoned for the development of an apartment complex, Westlake Apartments. This property was formerly located in Green Oak Township and annexed into the City, with a single-family residential zoning designation, and later conditionally rezoned to multiple-family residential. The proposed multiple family rezoning portion is consistent with, and will be an extension of, Westlake Apartments multiple family residential development.

c. Compatible Use. This conditional rezoning limits the 24.40 +/- acre portion of the property to be developed as multiple family residential units, 1.63+/- acres as single family residential lots, while the remaining 8.14+/- acre portion of the parcel will develop as low-rise industrial development. The City has the ability to permit a use that does not match the Future Land Use designation when it determines that the conditions proposed as part of the rezoning make the requested use compatible with surrounding land uses. The condition offered by the applicant does not affect the land use compatibility.

### 2. Compatibility of the site's physical, geological, hydrological, and other environmental features with the potential uses allowed in the proposed zoning district

The parcel is located adjacent to existing industrial sites as well as in close proximity to both single family and multiple family residential. The site contains over 9 acres of wetlands, however, is large enough to accommodate single and multiple family residential as well as industrial development. The wooded and wet area in the northeast area of the property should not limit the ability to develop the site.

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

<a id="p263"></a>
*[Page 263 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=263)* (read by OCR; check the original)

As a condition of rezoning, this property cannot develop into any other permitted or special land use other than the uses identified in the Conditional Rezoning Agreement and in a manner consistent with the Preliminary Site Plan.

### 3. Evidence the applicant cannot receive a reasonable return on investment through developing the property with one of the uses permitted under the current zoning

The applicant has provided a Market Study and analysis of current real estate trends and projections to support a reasonable return on investment as a residential property and refute a reasonable rate of return on large industrial projects. From the time period the property obtained site plan review until recently, the community, State, and Country endured an economic downturn. Michigan was especially affected by rising unemployment, property value decline and foreclosures, and population loss. Large scale industrial development at this location – on an unpaved, load limited road, Dixboro Road – has not occurred in the 20 years since the initial industrial park approval. The combination of both smaller industrial buildings and multiple family residential fills the residential and industrial use gap.

4. The compatibility of all the potential uses allowed in the proposed zoning district with surrounding uses and zoning in terms of land suitability, impacts on the environment, density, nature of use, traffic impacts, aesthetics, infrastructure, and potential influence on property values.

### a. Uses

Multiple Family Residential. The  $ \underline{\text{multiple family residential uses}} $ allowed in the RM-3 district are consistent with parcels in the vicinity (west of Dixboro Road in Green Oak Township and north of Ten Mile Road at Westlake Apartments and Charleston Park). By limiting the industrial uses to low impact uses, the industrial portion of the property will be compatible with the residential.

The intent of the RM-3, Multiple Residential Zoning District (Section 102-80) is:

○ is designed to provide for planned multiple-family development in limited locations where such land use is not incompatible with single-family residential uses. This district is further provided to accommodate independent and dependent housing facilities.

Single Family Residential. The single family uses allowed in the R-3 district are consistent with the existing single family residential uses on Lake Street.

The intent of the R-3, Single Residential Zoning District (Section 102-70) is:

○ is designed to be the most restrictive of the residential districts and to provide for an environment of predominantly low density, one family detached dwellings.

Industrial. The  $ \underline{\text{industrial development}} $ is consistent with the adjacent properties—Wastewater Treatment Plant and Michigan Seamless Tube—and are intended for research and design, laboratories, recreation, outdoor storage, light manufacturing, assembling, and light fabrication activities, whose external physical effects may be felt to some degree by surrounding properties. Noise from the existing operations (and future) as well as light, dust,

<a id="p264"></a>
*[Page 264 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=264)* (read by OCR; check the original)

and dirt may be a concern with neighboring residential uses, however, as existing industrial uses are existing, residents of the future multiple family residences will be aware of the neighboring conditions before deciding to reside in this area.

The intent of the Industrial Zoning District (Section 102-130):

○ Is to primarily accommodate wholesale activities, warehouses, and light industrial operations whose external, physical effects are minimal and in no manner affect in a detrimental way any of the surrounding properties/districts.

Since available industrial land in the city is limited, this district is also intended to accommodate uses that are office- or research-type, along with "business center" buildings that combine office and flexible industrial space.

It is further intended that the processing of raw material for shipment of bulk form, to be used in an industrial operation at another location, is not permitted.

b. Traffic Impacts. An increase in residential traffic and daily trips is anticipated, however, traffic created by heavy weight trucks and other industrial vehicles will be reduced than if developed as an entirely industrially zoned property. The applicant has provided a traffic analysis/impact study for the proposed development. Road modifications are proposed to accommodate the number of vehicles and to reduce traffic conflicts.

c. Aesthetics. The existing multiple family development at Westlake Apartments has high quality materials and architectural details. This proposed development will be a continuation of this same high quality.

d. Infrastructure. The applicant is aware that Dixboro Road lacks the necessary physical improvements to attract industrial development. As such the applicant is proposing to pave Dixboro Road from Lake Street to the southern end of the subject property. This will ensure that industrial vehicles are able to access the site.

The capacity of city infrastructure and services sufficient to accommodate the uses permitted in the requested district without compromising the "health, safety, and welfare" of the city.

The proposed rezoning will increase the demand for public services; however, water and sanitary services have been designed to accommodate additional development (previously designated as only industrial on this property which depending on the use may be substantial). The City Engineers and the DPW should work with the applicant to accurately estimate the demand for water and sanitary services.

e. Use Demand. The apparent demand for the types of uses permitted in the requested zoning district in the city in relation to the amount of land in the city currently zoned to accommodate the demand.

The City of South Lyon land use inventory is rich in residential development. Sixty-seven percent (67%) of the land within the City is zoned and/or used for residential purposes; however, industrially zoned and utilized land in the City accounts for 4.3%. Industrial land is a critical component of the City's land use system and is an important source of jobs, income, and services. Industrial uses, despite perceived challenges of noise, air quality, and truck

<a id="p265"></a>
*[Page 265 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=265)* (read by OCR; check the original)

traffic, are critical to the health and growth of the City. They boost the tax base and provide jobs that enable residents to stay in the South Lyon area. Given the lack of developable land in the City, the City must actively protect its remaining industrial districts.

As the industrial zoned property has remained undeveloped for decades, there are clear obstacles to developing it. Real estate brokers have indicated that the current condition of Dixboro Road (unpaved) presents a challenge for industrial users. As part of this development proposal, the applicant proposes to pave Dixboro Road from Lake Street to the south property line, increasing opportunities for industrial development of that area.

The rezoning shifts will result in a 2% decrease in industrial land and a 1.6% increase in multiple family residential.

<table><tr><td>Use</td><td>Acres</td><td>Percent of Total Land Area</td><td>Percent With Conditional Rezoned Development</td></tr><tr><td>Single Family</td><td>1186</td><td>59.1</td><td>59.1</td></tr><tr><td>Multiple Family</td><td>162</td><td>8</td><td>9.6</td></tr><tr><td>Commercial</td><td>87</td><td>4.3</td><td>4.3</td></tr><tr><td>Industrial</td><td>87</td><td>4.3</td><td>2.8</td></tr><tr><td>Governmental</td><td>256</td><td>12.8</td><td>12.8</td></tr><tr><td>Parks and Open Space</td><td>154</td><td>7.7</td><td>7.7</td></tr><tr><td>Transportation</td><td>56</td><td>2.8</td><td>2.8</td></tr><tr><td>Undeveloped</td><td>18</td><td>.9</td><td>.9</td></tr></table>

f. Most Appropriate Zoning. Where a rezoning is reasonable given the above criteria, a determination shall be made that the requested zoning district is more appropriate than another district or amending the list of permitted or special land uses within a district.

The City's master plan has a goal to "provide housing to match the varied needs and income levels of the present and future population." Currently multiple family residential comprise eight percent (8%) of the total existing land use in the City, and twelve percent (12%) of all residential. The proposed housing works toward the goal of providing high quality housing for a diverse population with varying incomes, community gathering spaces, and safe, convenient routes and access.

A Master Plan goal for Industrial land is “to strive for full occupancy within the City’s Industrial Parks.” The property to the south of our subject site is a vacant industrial park with infrastructure in place and waiting for build out and tenancy. Designating a portion of the site for low intensity industrial uses will further the goal for industrial growth.

This conditional rezoning limits the property to be developed as multiple-family residential and industrial development and excludes all other permitted uses. The City has the ability to

<a id="p266"></a>
*[Page 266 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=266)* (read by OCR; check the original)

permit a use that does not match the Future Land Use designation when it determines that the conditions proposed make the requested use compatible with surrounding land uses.

## REZONING RECOMMENDATION

Following a public hearing for a proposed zoning amendment, the Planning Commission makes a recommendation to the City Council based upon the criteria listed in Section 102-213, Criteria for Amendment of the Official Zoning Map (rezoning).

In addition, following a public hearing to consider a Conditional Rezoning, the Planning Commission shall consider and address in written findings whether a proposed conditional rezoning:

1. Is consistent with the intent of this article.

2. Bears a reasonable and rational connection and/or benefit to the property being proposed for rezoning.

3. Is necessary to ensure that the property develops in such a way that protects the surrounding neighborhood.

4. Leads to a better development than would have been likely if the property had been rezoned without a conditional rezoning, or if the property were left to develop under the existing zoning classification.

5. Is clearly in the public interest.

<a id="p267"></a>
*[Page 267 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=267)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## Part 2:

## Preliminary Site Plan Review

A Conditional Rezoning ties a specific preliminary site plan/conceptual plan to the rezoning of the property. No other use or site plan development may occur on a conditionally rezoned parcel other than that shown in the Preliminary Site Plan, approved as part of the Conditional Rezoning Agreement.

The Preliminary Site Plan approval provides general acceptance of the road layout, lot (unit) sizes, open space, preliminary landscaping, and housing designs. After a public hearing has been held for the Conditional Rezoning and Preliminary Plan review, a recommendation from the Planning Commission to the City Council is made. If the Conditional Rezoning and corresponding Preliminary Site Plan are approved by the City Council, the applicant will return to the Planning Commission for Final Site Plan review and approval. All of the requirements for site plan submissions in Article IV of the Zoning Ordinance must be complied with at that time.

As a part of the Conditional Rezoning Review, we have reviewed the Preliminary Site Plan for development of both multiple family residential and industrial, to be built in three (3) phases (and as an extension of the existing West Lake Apartments, identified as Phase 1 on the site plan).

The opinions in this report are based on a review of the site plan submitted by the applicant and conformance to City plans and ordinance standards. Please note that the applicant and their design professionals shall be responsible for the accuracy and validity of information presented with the application and on the site plan.

## PRELIMINARY SITE PLAN REVIEW COMMENTS

1. Overall Site Development. The applicant is proposing to conditionally rezone 24.40+/- acres of the industrially zoned parcel to RM-3 to develop 252 new multiple family apartment units and rezone 1.63+/- acres to R-3 to develop three (3) single family residential lots fronting on Lake Street, east of Dixboro, and use the remaining 8.14+/- acres to develop up to 63,742 square feet of industrial. The multiple family residential use will be accessed via two (2) curb cuts on Dixboro and a divided entrance on Lake Street (constructed at later phases); the single family lots will be accessed via curb cuts/private driveways along Lake Street; and the industrial use is accessed via a separate curb cut on Dixboro that accesses the industrial portion only.

For the residential component, the applicant is additionally proposing 28 garages, 4 car charging stations, a 3,000 square foot club house, covered patio, and outdoor pool, walking paths, gazebos, community garden, pickle ball courts, bike racks and a dog park. If approved as envisioned, the Westlake project will include three (3) phases of residential development each with 84 units.

a. Residential Density. The applicant is proposing 252 units over 24.40 $ \pm $ acres which equals  $ \sim $10 units per acre. With a site area over 1 million square feet and a total of 584 bedrooms, the site area per bedroom is approximately 1,949 square feet per bedroom. Unit sizes are between 600 and 664 square feet for a one (1) bedroom unit; 768 to 947 square feet for a

<a id="p268"></a>
*[Page 268 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=268)* (read by OCR; check the original)

two (2) bedroom unit; and 936 square feet for a three (3) bedroom unit. Three (3) R-3, single family outlots on West Lake are proposed.

b. Access and Circulation. Twenty-seven (27) foot drive aisles circulate the site and provide access to all phases and buildings. The residential use will be accessed via two (2) curb cuts on Dixboro and a divided entrance on Lake Street (constructed at a later phase). The relocation of the entrance off of Lake Street is to minimize the number of traffic conflicts at Dixboro and Lake (10 Mile). Funneling residential traffic to the main boulevard entrance will reduce the number of residential vehicles turning from Dixboro. The industrial use is accessed via a separate curb cut on Dixboro- with a horseshow style drive- accessing the industrial portion only. There is no vehicular connection between the residential and the industrial uses.

Parking for both the residential and industrial development:

Per the Zoning Ordinance, the parking requirements for the multiple family residential total 596 spaces.  $ \underline{\text{This amount of parking has been found to be excessive based on the existing}} $

parking at Westlake Apartments and a traffic study prepared by Fleis and Vandenbrink. As proposed, the number of parking spaces has been reduced by 166 spaces. If the Planning Commission agrees with the traffic study and determines that a reduction in parking is warranted, the rezoning condition offered by the applicant should be accepted.

Parking standards for the “industrial” uses vary. Depending on the use, it may be reviewed as industrial or even recreation (a gym or dance studio). At this time, 223 spaces are provided for industrial development. Sixty-seven (67) parking spaces are proposed for the Phase 1 Industrial building. Section 102-251(i) permits a 20% increase over the amount of required parking. As the proposed industrial buildings have no known users at this time, the applicant is proposing an increase in parking of 200%. The uncertainty of users at this time makes the parking calculations difficult. Acceptance of the offered Rezoning Condition is required to permit this increase in parking.

To prevent excessive parking, the Planning Commission may authorize the Planner and Engineer to administratively evaluate and approve the required parking as each building and unit is built or occupied or when a site plan is submitted for the specific configuration of the remaining Light Industrial district, the applicant may submit a traffic study to establish a basis for the appropriate number of spaces. If the remaining "approved" amount of parking far exceeds the demand, this parking may be defined as "deferred" parking on as-built plans and be constructed when there is a demonstrated need.

### e. Woodlands, Wetlands, Landscaping (Greenbelts and Buffers)

1.  $ \underline{\text{Woodlands}} $. A woodlands survey has been conducted. Fifty percent (50%) of the existing trees will be preserved on site and over 627 inches diameter at breast height (DBH) will be replaced throughout the site, meeting ordinance standards.

2. Wetlands. There is a 9-acre wetland, regulated by Michigan Department of Environment, Great Lakes, and Energy, EGLE, on this property. A wetland permit is required for the construction of the single family lots on West Lake as well as the relocated residential entrance and proposed concrete walk along West Lake Street frontage. It should be noted that the applicant appears to have designed the site to minimize impacts of the development on the wetlands. The applicant is not proposing the wetlands to be billed for the re-located entrance, but rather a bridge with piers. The applicant is maintaining a minimum 50-foot setback from the wetland areas, as required.

3.  $ \underline{\text{Landscaping}} $.

(a) Greenbelt. Per section 102-276 Design Standards, a 35-foot greenbelt along Dixboro Road and between access drives and adjacent land uses is required for the multiple family residential use and a 20-foot greenbelt along Dixboro is required for the industrial use. As access drives circulate though the existing Westlake Apartments and connect directly into the new development, the required greenbelt is not met

<a id="p271"></a>
*[Page 271 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=271)* (read by OCR; check the original)

between the existing apartments and the proposed development. This is a rezoning condition offered as part of the plan and should be accepted as shown on the preliminary site plan.

(b) Street Trees. The landscape plan provides the required street trees and plantings. Nine (9) street trees along West Lake and Dixboro are proposed; 99 parking lot trees are proposed; and 209 woodland replacement trees are proposed. This meets ordinance standards and will be reviewed more in depth (for species variety and design) during Final site plan review.

(c) Buffers. Section 102-276 (c) Buffers requires that a buffer be provided between the site/uses and all adjacent properties.

<table><tr><td>Zone or Proposed Use</td><td>Adjacent Zone or Use</td><td>Required Buffer</td><td>Provided</td><td>Comment</td></tr><tr><td>Multiple Family Residential</td><td>Industrial</td><td>30-foot minimum width along with 2 canopy trees (or 1 canopy and 1 evergreen) plus 4 shrubs per 20 linear feet and including a wall or berm.</td><td>Not dimensioned</td><td>Rezoning Condition Offered</td></tr><tr><td>Industrial</td><td>Multiple Family Residential</td><td>30-foot minimum width along with 2 canopy trees (or 1 canopy and 1 evergreen) plus 4 shrubs per 20 linear feet and including a wall or berm.</td><td>Not dimensioned</td><td>Deviation approval is required</td></tr></table>

f. Parking Lot/Circulation. The proposed parking lot and drive aisles are proposed to be paved with asphalt with all islands curbed, thereby meeting ordinance requirements. Sidewalk connections from the parking areas to building entrances are provided and meet ordinance requirements.

The access drives through the existing Westlake Apartments continues and connects into this proposed new development. This effectively connects the multiple family phases, reduces curb cuts on Dixboro and Lake Streets, reduces impervious surface, and creates more efficient traffic circulation throughout the site. A cross-access easement between the two parcels is required and a condition of site plan approval.

### g. Building Elevations

1.  $ \underline{\text{Apartments}} $: As a continuation of the existing Westlake Apartments, the applicant is proposing the same architecture and use of durable, exterior building materials including

<a id="p272"></a>
*[Page 272 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/1-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20Part%201%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202401181517400#page=272)* (read by OCR; check the original)

cultured stone accents, high quality composite siding, high efficiency and quality rated windows, and attractive solid core exterior doors. Building walls exceeding 50 feet in length are broken up with balconies, material, and color changes. Large windows and balcony doors also break up the building mass with transparency.

The club house design mimics a single-family home, with pitched rooflines, dormers, windows, a front door, and materials matching the apartment buildings- stone and siding.

2.  $ \underline{\text{Industrial:}} $ The industrial building architecture has clean lines and uses a variety of durable materials – woodgrain aluminum siding, insulated metal panels, glass, and CMU on the rear of the buildings (south elevations). The rear facades are broken up by architectural details and garage doors. Each building will be built as a "spec" building with tenant build outs.

The industrial building possesses architectural variety that enhances the overall cohesive community, mixed-use character. Window areas make up at least 20 percent of the exterior wall area facing the principal street(s) from which access is gained. The overhead garage doors do not face a public street or residential district. The flat roofs are designed to reduce the apparent exterior mass of a building,

Architectural methods are used to conceal flat roof tops and mechanical equipment. A parapet is proposed around the building with a height maximum of 24 feet 8 inches. While not shown, any mechanical equipment will require screening.

3. Single family lots on Lake Street. Elevations have not been provided for the three single family lots on West Lake Street at this time. Section 102-39 Residential Development Regulations for Infill Housing for Existing Neighborhoods, regulates the character of new infill housing development within a 300-foot defined area of the city which contain traditional and historic exterior design elements. The purpose of these regulations is to promote harmony in neighborhoods between new housing units and the existing building by assuring that new construction is of suitable character in terms of site layout, building dimensions, architectural design, and building materials. New house plans for these lots will be reviewed against this provision.

h. Emergency Access. Emergency access is provided with a boulevard curb cut on West Lake Street and three curb cuts on Dixboro.

i. Other Department and Agency Review. Site plan approval must be conditioned upon review and approval from other applicable consultants, departments, and agencies, including the Road Commission for Oakland County, EGLE, and Oakland County Drain Commissioner.

Section 102-215 Conditional Rezoning (a)(2) states that a “conditional rezoning may include limitations on the uses permitted on the property, specify lower or varying density or less intensity of development and use, or may impose more restrictive measures on the location, size, height, or other measure for building

structures, improvements, setbacks, landscaping, buffers, design, architecture, and other features than otherwise would be permitted on the property."
